**Meningeal branch** 起源於 **mandibular nerve [V₃]** 的內側，向上與 **maxillary artery** 發出的 **middle meningeal artery** 伴行，通過 **foramen spinosum** 重新進入顱腔（圖 8.144）。它支配硬腦膜（主要為中顱窩），也支配與中耳相通的乳突氣房。

---

### Nerve to Medial Pterygoid（內翼肌神經）

**Nerve to the medial pterygoid** 同樣起源於 **mandibular nerve [V₃]** 的內側（圖 8.144）。它下行進入並支配 **medial pterygoid muscle** 的深面。在其起源處附近有兩個小分支：

- 一個支配 **tensor veli palatini**。
- 另一個向上支配 **tensor tympani muscle**，該肌肉位於顳骨中與咽鼓管平行的小骨管內。

---

### Buccal Nerve（頰神經）

**Buccal nerve** 是 **mandibular nerve [V₃]** 前幹的分支（圖 8.144）。它主要為感覺神經，但也可攜帶支配 **lateral pterygoid muscle** 及部分 **temporalis muscle** 的運動纖維。

**Buccal nerve** 在 **lateral pterygoid** 上下頭之間向外側通過，隨後在 **temporalis muscle** 附著點前緣繞過下頜支前緣下行，常穿過 **temporalis** 的肌腱。它繼續進入頰部，位於 **buccinator muscle** 外側，支配相鄰皮膚與口腔黏膜及下磨牙的頰側齦的一般感覺神經。

---

### Masseteric Nerve（咀嚼肌神經）

**Masseteric nerve** 是 **mandibular nerve [V₃]** 前幹的分支（圖 8.144；另見圖 8.137）。它向外側越過 **lateral pterygoid muscle**，穿過下頜切跡，進入並支配 **masseter muscle**。

---

### Deep Temporal Nerves（深顳神經）

**Deep temporal nerves** 通常為兩條，起源於 **mandibular nerve [V₃]** 的前幹（圖 8.144；另見圖 8.140）。它們向外側越過 **lateral pterygoid muscle** 上方，繞過 **infratemporal crest** 在顳窩向上走行，從深面支配 **temporalis muscle**。

---

### Nerve to Lateral Pterygoid（外翼肌神經）

**Nerve to the lateral pterygoid** 可直接起源於 **mandibular nerve [V₃]** 的前幹，或起源於其 **buccal branch**（圖 8.144）。從起源處，它直接進入 **lateral pterygoid muscle** 的深面。

---

### Auriculotemporal Nerve（耳顳神經）

**Auriculotemporal nerve** 是 **mandibular nerve [V₃]** 後幹的第一個分支，起源時有兩根，繞過向上走向 **foramen spinosum** 的 **middle meningeal artery** 向後走行（圖 8.145）。

**Auriculotemporal nerve** 首先在 **tensor veli palatini muscle** 與 **lateral pterygoid muscle** 上頭之間通過，隨後在 **sphenomandibular ligament** 與下頜骨頸之間通過。它繞過下頜骨頸向外側，隨後在顳下頜關節與耳朵之間、腮腺深處向上走行。

**Auriculotemporal nerve** 的終支支配顳部大面積皮膚的一般感覺。此外，**auriculotemporal nerve** 也支配外耳、外耳道、鼓膜及顳下頜關節的感覺神經。它還傳導來自 **glossopharyngeal nerve [IX]** 的節後副交感神經至腮腺。

---

### Lingual Nerve（舌神經）

**Lingual nerve** 是 **mandibular nerve [V₃]** 後幹的主要感覺分支（圖 8.145A、B）。它傳導舌頭前三分之二、口腔底黏膜及下頜牙齒相關齦的一般感覺。

**Lingual nerve** 在顳下窩高處與 **facial nerve [VII]** 的分支 **chorda tympani** 會合（圖 8.145C），後者攜帶：

- 舌頭前三分之一的味覺，及
- 口腔裂以下所有唾液腺的副交感纖維。

**Lingual nerve** 首先在 **tensor veli palatini muscle** 與 **lateral pterygoid muscle** 之間下行，在該處與 **chorda tympani nerve** 會合，隨後下行越過 **medial pterygoid muscle** 的外側面進入口腔。

**Lingual nerve** 進入口腔時，介於 **mylohyoid muscle** 後附著於 **mylohyoid line** 處與 **superior constrictor of the pharynx** 附著於 **pterygomandibular raphe** 處之間。

當 **lingual nerve** 進入口腔底時，它位於下頜骨內側面、緊鄰最後一顆磨牙根方的淺溝中。在此位置，它可通過口腔黏膜觸及，在處理磨牙及齦時有受損風險（圖 8.145C）。

**Lingual nerve** 進入舌頭時，在 **hyoglossus muscle** 的外側面，並在此處附著於 **submandibular ganglion**。該神經節是攜帶自顳下窩進入口腔底的節前副交感纖維與節後副交感纖維發生突觸的部位（見圖 8.146）。

> **臨床重點**：Lingual Nerve Injury（舌神經損傷）
>
> 在 **chorda tympani** 會合處近端的舌神經損傷，會導致舌頭前三分之二、口腔黏膜、齦、下唇及下巴的一般感覺喪失。
>
> 若舌神經損傷部位在與 **chorda tympani** 會合處的遠端，則口腔裂以下唾液腺的分泌及舌頭前三分之一的味覺也會喪失。

---

### Inferior Alveolar Nerve（下齒槽神經）

與 **lingual nerve** 相同，**inferior alveolar nerve** 是 **mandibular nerve [V₃]** 後幹的主要感覺分支（圖 8.145A-C）。除了支配所有下頜牙齒及大部分相關齦外，也支配下唇黏膜與皮膚及下巴的皮膚。它有一個運動分支，支配 **mylohyoid muscle** 與 **digastric muscle** 的前腹。

**Inferior alveolar nerve** 起源於 **lateral pterygoid muscle** 深層、與 **lingual nerve** 相伴的 **mandibular nerve [V₃]** 後幹。它在 **medial pterygoid muscle** 的外側面下行，穿過 **sphenomandibular ligament** 與下頜支之間，隨後通過 **mandibular foramen** 進入下頜管。

就在進入 **mandibular foramen** 之前，它發出 **nerve to the mylohyoid**（圖 8.145C），該神經位於孔下方 **mylohyoid groove** 中，繼續向前在口腔底下行，支配 **mylohyoid muscle** 與 **digastric muscle** 的前腹。

**Inferior alveolar nerve** 在下頜骨的 **mandibular canal** 中向前走行。**Mandibular canal** 及其內容物位於磨牙牙根下方，牙根有時可彎曲繞過神經管，使這些牙齒的拔除變得困難。

**Inferior alveolar nerve** 支配三顆磨牙及第二前磨牙及其唇側齦的分支，隨後分為兩個終支：

- **Incisive nerve**：繼續在 **mandibular canal** 中走行，支配第一前磨牙、門齒及犬齒及相關齦。
- **Mental nerve**：通過 **mental foramen** 離開下頜骨，支配下唇及下巴（圖 8.145A、B）。**Mental nerve** 可觸及，有時在鄰近前磨牙根方的口腔黏膜上可見。

---

## Chorda Tympani and Lesser Petrosal Nerve（鼓索神經與岩淺小神經）

兩個腦神經的分支在顳下窩中與 **mandibular nerve [V₃]** 的分支會合（圖 8.146）。這些是 **facial nerve [VII]** 的 **chorda tympani** 分支，及中耳 **tympanic plexus** 的分支 **lesser petrosal nerve**，後者起源於 **glossopharyngeal nerve [IX]** 的分支（見圖 8.121，p. 964）。

---

### Chorda Tympani（鼓索神經）

**Chorda tympani**（圖 8.146）傳導舌頭前三分之一的味覺及口腔裂以下所有唾液腺的副交感神經支配。

**Chorda tympani** 起源於顳骨內的 **facial nerve [VII]**，與中耳乳突壁相關。它向前穿過一個小管，進入中耳外側面。它繼續向前上方向通過中耳時，與錘骨柄之間隔有鼓膜。它通過 **petrotympanic fissure** 的內側端離開中耳，進入顳下窩，在蝶骨棘內側及 **lateral pterygoid muscle** 外側下行，與 **lingual nerve** 會合。

**Chorda tympani** 中攜帶的節前副交感纖維在口腔底處懸於 **lingual nerve** 上的 **submandibular ganglion** 處與節後副交感纖維發生突觸（圖 8.146）。

節後副交感纖維離開 **submandibular ganglion**：

- 重新進入 **lingual nerve**，隨其終支到達目標組織，或
- 直接從 **submandibular ganglion** 進入腺體（圖 8.146）。

味覺（一般軀體感覺）纖維不通過神經節，隨 **lingual nerve** 的終支分布。

---

### Lesser Petrosal Nerve（岩淺小神經）

**Lesser petrosal nerve** 起源於中耳的 **tympanic plexus**，而該叢起源於 **glossopharyngeal nerve [IX]** 的分支。它傳導節前副交感纖維至 **otic ganglion**（圖 8.146）。

**Otic ganglion** 位於 **mandibular nerve [V₃]** 內側。來自 **lesser petrosal nerve** 的節前副交感纖維在此與節後纖維發生突觸。節後纖維隨後通過 **auriculotemporal nerve** 到達目標組織，主要是腮腺。

---

### Parasympathetic Innervation Summary（副交感神經支配總結）

| 神經 | 起源 | 目標 |
|------|------|------|
| **Chorda tympani** | **Facial nerve [VII]** | 口腔裂以下唾液腺（submandibular、sublingual gland）、舌前三分之二味覺 |
| **Lesser petrosal nerve** | **Glossopharyngeal nerve [IX]** | 腮腺（通過 otic ganglion 及 auriculotemporal nerve） |

> **臨床重點**：下頜神經 [V₃] 的感覺與運動功能完整性對咀嚼、口腔感覺及唾液分泌至關重要。在顳下窩及下頜區域的手術中，了解這些結構的解剖關係可避免術後神經損傷。

# Gray's Anatomy — Head & Neck
## Segment 33: Regional Anatomy — Temporal and Infratemporal Fossae

---

## Lesser Petrosal Nerve

The **lesser petrosal nerve** carries mainly parasympathetic fibers destined for the **parotid gland** (Fig. 8.146). The preganglionic parasympathetic fibers are located in the **glossopharyngeal nerve [IX]** as it exits the jugular foramen at the base of the skull. Branching from the glossopharyngeal nerve [IX] either within or immediately outside the jugular foramen is the **tympanic nerve** (Fig. 8.146B).

The tympanic nerve reenters the temporal bone through a small foramen on the ridge of bone separating the jugular foramen from the carotid canal and ascends through a small bony canal (inferior tympanic canaliculus) to the promontory located on the labyrinthine (medial) wall of the middle ear. Here it participates in the formation of the **tympanic plexus**. The lesser petrosal nerve is a branch of this plexus (Fig. 8.146B).

The lesser petrosal nerve contains mainly preganglionic parasympathetic fibers. It leaves the middle ear and enters the middle cranial fossa through a small opening on the anterior surface of the petrous part of the **temporal bone** just lateral and inferior to the opening for the **greater petrosal nerve**, a branch of the **facial nerve [VII]**. The lesser petrosal nerve then passes medially and descends through the **foramen ovale** with the **mandibular nerve [V3]**.

In the infratemporal fossa, the preganglionic parasympathetic fibers synapse with cell bodies of postganglionic parasympathetic fibers in the **otic ganglion** located on the medial side of the mandibular nerve [V3] around the origin of the nerve to the **medial pterygoid**. Postganglionic parasympathetic fibers leave the otic ganglion and join the **auriculotemporal nerve**, which carries them to the parotid gland.

> **臨床重點**：Dental Anesthesia
> 
> **Inferior alveolar nerve** anesthesia is widely practiced by most dentists. The inferior alveolar nerve is one of the largest branches of the **mandibular nerve [V3]**, carries the sensory branches from the teeth and mandible, and receives sensory information from the skin over most of the mandible.
> 
> The inferior alveolar nerve passes into the mandibular canal, courses through the body of the mandible, and eventually emerges through the **mental foramen** into the chin.
> 
> Dental procedures require perineuronal infiltration of the inferior alveolar nerve by local anesthetic. To anesthetize this nerve the needle is placed lateral to the anterior arch of the fauces (palatoglossal arch) in the oral cavity and is advanced along the medial border around the inferior third of the ramus of the mandible so that anesthetic can be deposited in this region.
> 
> It is also possible to anesthetize the **infra-orbital nerve** and **buccal nerve**, depending on where the anesthesia is needed.

---

## Maxillary Artery

The **maxillary artery** is the largest branch of the **external carotid artery** in the neck and is a major source of blood supply for the nasal cavity, the lateral wall and roof of the oral cavity, all teeth, and the **dura mater** in the cranial cavity. It passes through and supplies the infratemporal fossa and then enters the **pterygopalatine fossa**, where it gives origin to terminal branches (Fig. 8.147).

The maxillary artery originates within the substance of the **parotid gland** and then passes forward, between the neck of the mandible and **sphenomandibular ligament**, into the infratemporal fossa. It ascends obliquely through the infratemporal fossa to enter the pterygopalatine fossa by passing through the **pterygomaxillary fissure**. This part of the vessel may pass either lateral or medial to the lower head of the **lateral pterygoid**. If it passes medial to the lower head, the maxillary artery then loops laterally between the upper and lower heads of the lateral pterygoid to access the pterygomaxillary fissure.

### Branches

Branches of the maxillary artery are as follows (Fig. 8.147):

- The **first part** of the maxillary artery (the part between the neck of the mandible and the sphenomandibular ligament) gives origin to two major branches (the **middle meningeal** and **inferior alveolar arteries**) and a number of smaller branches (deep auricular, anterior tympanic, and accessory meningeal).
- The **second part** of the maxillary artery (the part related to the **lateral pterygoid muscle**) gives origin to deep temporal, masseteric, buccal, and pterygoid branches, which course with branches of the mandibular nerve [V3].
- The **third part** of the maxillary artery is in the pterygopalatine fossa.

### Middle Meningeal Artery

The **middle meningeal artery** ascends vertically from the maxillary artery and passes through the **foramen spinosum** to enter the cranial cavity (Fig. 8.147). In the infratemporal fossa, it passes superiorly between the sphenomandibular ligament on the medial side and the lateral pterygoid muscle on the lateral side. Just inferior to the foramen spinosum, it passes between the two roots of the **auriculotemporal nerve** at their origin from the mandibular nerve [V3] (Fig. 8.147).

The middle meningeal artery is the largest of the meningeal vessels and supplies much of the dura mater, bone, and related bone marrow of the cranial cavity walls.

> **臨床重點**：Extradural Hematoma
> 
> Within the cranial cavity, the middle meningeal artery and its branches travel in the periosteal (outer) layer of **dura mater**, which is tightly adherent to the bony walls. As major branches of the middle meningeal artery pass superiorly up the walls of the cranial cavity, they can be damaged by lateral blows to the head. When the vessels are torn, the leaking blood, which is under arterial pressure, slowly separates the dura mater from its attachment to the bone, resulting in an **extradural hematoma**.

### Inferior Alveolar Artery

The **inferior alveolar artery** descends from the maxillary artery to enter the **mandibular foramen** and canal with the inferior alveolar nerve (Fig. 8.147). It is distributed with the inferior alveolar nerve and supplies all lower teeth, and contributes to the supply of the buccal gingivae, chin, and lower lip.

Before entering the mandible, the inferior alveolar artery gives origin to a small **mylohyoid branch**, which accompanies the nerve to the mylohyoid.

### Deep Auricular, Anterior Tympanic, and Accessory Meningeal Arteries

The **deep auricular**, **anterior tympanic**, and **accessory meningeal arteries** are small branches from the first part of the maxillary artery and contribute to the blood supply of the external acoustic meatus, deep surface of the tympanic membrane, and cranial dura mater, respectively.

The accessory meningeal branch also contributes small branches to surrounding muscles in the infratemporal fossa before ascending through the foramen ovale into the cranial cavity to supply the dura mater.

### Branches from the Second Part

**Deep temporal arteries**, usually two in number, originate from the second part of the maxillary artery and travel with the deep temporal nerves to supply the **temporalis muscle** in the temporal fossa (Fig. 8.147).

Numerous **pterygoid arteries** also originate from the second part of the maxillary artery and supply the pterygoid muscles.

The **masseteric artery**, also from the second part of the maxillary artery, accompanies the masseteric nerve laterally through the **mandibular notch** to supply the masseter muscle.

The **buccal artery** is distributed with the buccal nerve and supplies skin, muscle, and oral mucosa of the cheek.

---

## Pterygoid Plexus

The **pterygoid plexus** is a network of veins between the **medial** and **lateral pterygoid muscles**, and between the lateral pterygoid and temporalis muscles (Fig. 8.148).

Veins that drain regions supplied by arteries branching from the maxillary artery in the infratemporal fossa and pterygopalatine fossa connect with the pterygoid plexus. These tributary veins include those that drain the nasal cavity, roof and lateral wall of the oral cavity, all teeth, muscles of the infratemporal fossa, paranasal sinuses, and nasopharynx. In addition, the **inferior ophthalmic vein** from the orbit can drain through the inferior orbital fissure into the pterygoid plexus.

> **臨床重點**：Emissary Veins and Infection Spread
> 
> Significantly, small **emissary veins** often connect the pterygoid plexus in the infratemporal fossa to the **cavernous sinus** in the cranial cavity. These emissary veins, which pass through the foramen ovale, through the cartilage that fills the foramen lacerum, and through a small sphenoidal foramen on the medial side of the lateral plate of the pterygoid process at the base of the skull, are a route by which infections can spread into the cranial cavity from structures, such as the teeth, that are drained by the pterygoid plexus.
> 
> Also, because there are no valves in veins of the head and neck, anesthetic inadvertently injected under pressure into veins of the pterygoid plexus can backflow into tissues or into the cranial cavity.

The pterygoid plexus connects:

- **Posteriorly**, via a short **maxillary vein**, with the **retromandibular vein** in the neck; and
- **Anteriorly**, via a **deep facial vein**, with the **facial vein** on the face.

---

## Pterygopalatine Fossa

The **pterygopalatine fossa** is an inverted teardrop-shaped space between bones on the lateral side of the skull immediately posterior to the **maxilla** (Fig. 8.149).

Although small in size, the pterygopalatine fossa communicates via fissures and foramina in its walls with the:

- Middle cranial fossa,
- Infratemporal fossa,
- Floor of the orbit,
- Lateral wall of the nasal cavity,
- Oropharynx, and
- Roof of the oral cavity.

Because of its strategic location, the pterygopalatine fossa is a major site of distribution for the **maxillary nerve [V2]** and for the terminal part of the **maxillary artery**. It also contains the **pterygopalatine ganglion** where preganglionic parasympathetic fibers originating in the **facial nerve [VII]** synapse with postganglionic parasympathetic fibers and these fibers, along with sympathetic fibers originating from the **T1 spinal cord level** join branches of the maxillary nerve [V2].

All the upper teeth receive their innervation and blood supply from the maxillary nerve [V2] and the terminal part of the maxillary artery, respectively, that pass through the pterygopalatine fossa.

### Skeletal Framework

The walls of the pterygopalatine fossa are formed by parts of the **palatine**, **maxilla**, and **sphenoid bones** (Fig. 8.149):

- The **anterior wall** is formed by the posterior surface of the maxilla.
- The **medial wall** is formed by the lateral surface of the palatine bone.
- The **posterior wall and roof** are formed by parts of the sphenoid bone.

### Sphenoid Bone

The part of the sphenoid bone that contributes to the formation of the pterygopalatine fossa is the anterosuperior surface of the **pterygoid process** (Fig. 8.150). Opening onto this surface are two large foramina:

- The **maxillary nerve [V2]** passes through the most lateral and superior of these—the **foramen rotundum**—which communicates posteriorly with the middle cranial fossa (Fig. 8.150B).
- The **greater petrosal nerve** from the facial nerve [VII] and sympathetic fibers from the internal carotid plexus join to form the **nerve of the pterygoid canal** that passes forward into the pterygopalatine fossa through the more medial and inferior foramen—the anterior opening of the **pterygoid canal**.

### Pterygoid Canal

The **pterygoid canal** (Fig. 8.150A) is a bony canal running horizontally through the root of the pterygoid process of the sphenoid bone. It opens anteriorly into the pterygopalatine fossa. Posteriorly it continues through the cartilage filling the foramen lacerum and opens into the middle cranial fossa just anteroinferior to the **internal carotid artery** as the vessel enters the cranial cavity through the carotid canal (Fig. 8.150B).

### Gateways

Seven foramina and fissures provide apertures through which structures enter and leave the pterygopalatine fossa (Fig. 8.151):

- The **foramen rotundum** and **pterygoid canal** communicate with the middle cranial fossa and open onto the posterior wall.
- A small **palatovaginal canal** opens onto the posterior wall and leads to the nasopharynx.
- The **palatine canal** leads to the roof of the oral cavity (hard palate) and opens inferiorly.
- The **sphenopalatine foramen** opens onto the lateral wall of the nasal cavity and is in the medial wall.
- The lateral aspect of the pterygopalatine fossa is continuous with the infratemporal fossa via a large gap (the **pterygomaxillary fissure**) between the posterior surface of the maxilla and pterygoid process of the sphenoid bone.
- The superior aspect of the anterior wall of the fossa opens into the floor of the orbit via the **inferior orbital fissure**.

### Contents

The **maxillary nerve [V2]** and terminal part of the **maxillary artery** enter and branch within the pterygopalatine fossa. In addition, the **nerve of the pterygoid canal** enters the fossa carrying:

- Preganglionic parasympathetic fibers from the greater petrosal branch of the facial nerve [VII]; and
- Postganglionic sympathetic fibers from the deep petrosal branch of the carotid plexus.

The preganglionic parasympathetic fibers synapse in the **pterygopalatine ganglion** and both the sympathetic and postganglionic parasympathetic fibers pass with branches of the maxillary nerve [V2] out of the fossa and into adjacent regions.

In addition to nerves and arteries, veins and lymphatics also pass through the pterygopalatine fossa.

---

## Maxillary Nerve [V2]

The **maxillary nerve [V2]** is purely sensory. It originates from the **trigeminal ganglion** in the cranial cavity, exits the middle cranial fossa, and enters the pterygopalatine fossa through the **foramen rotundum** (Fig. 8.152). It passes anteriorly through the fossa and exits as the **infra-orbital nerve** through the inferior orbital fissure.

While passing through the pterygopalatine fossa, the maxillary nerve [V2] gives rise to the **zygomatic nerve**, the **posterior superior alveolar nerve**, and two **ganglionic branches** (Fig. 8.152). The two ganglionic branches originate from its inferior surface and pass through the pterygopalatine ganglion.

Postganglionic parasympathetic fibers, arising in the pterygopalatine ganglion, join the general sensory branches of the maxillary nerve [V2] in the pterygopalatine ganglion, as do postganglionic sympathetic fibers from the carotid plexus. The three types of fibers leave the ganglion as **orbital**, **palatine**, **nasal**, and **pharyngeal branches**.

### Orbital Branches

The **orbital branches** are small and pass through the inferior orbital fissure to contribute to the supply of the orbital wall and of the sphenoidal and ethmoidal sinuses.

### Greater and Lesser Palatine Nerves

The **greater** and **lesser palatine nerves** (Fig. 8.152) pass inferiorly from the pterygopalatine ganglion, enter and pass through the palatine canal, and enter the oral surface of the palate through the greater and lesser palatine foramina.

The greater palatine nerve passes forward on the roof of the oral cavity to innervate mucosa and glands of the hard palate and the adjacent gingiva, almost as far forward as the incisor teeth.

In the palatine canal, the greater palatine nerve gives origin to **posterior inferior nasal nerves**, which pass medially through small foramina in the perpendicular plate of the palatine bone and contribute to the innervation of the lateral nasal wall.

After passing through the lesser palatine foramen, the lesser palatine nerve passes posteriorly to supply the soft palate.

### Nasal Nerves

The **nasal nerves** (Fig. 8.152), approximately seven in number, pass medially through the sphenopalatine foramen to enter the nasal cavity. Most pass anteriorly to supply the lateral wall of the nasal cavity, while others pass across the roof to supply the medial wall.

One of the nerves passing across the roof to supply the medial wall of the nasal cavity (the **nasopalatine nerve**) is the largest of the nasal nerves and passes anteriorly down the nasal septum, through the incisive canal and fossa in the hard palate to enter the roof of the oral cavity and supply mucosa, gingiva, and glands adjacent to the incisor teeth.

### Pharyngeal Nerve

The **pharyngeal nerve** (Fig. 8.152) passes posteriorly from the pterygopalatine ganglion, and leaves the fossa through the **palatovaginal canal**, which it then exits to supply the mucosa and glands of the nasopharynx.

### Zygomatic Nerve

The **zygomatic nerve** (Fig. 8.152) originates directly from the maxillary nerve [V2] in the pterygopalatine fossa, which it leaves to enter the orbit through the inferior orbital fissure. It passes forward on the lateral orbital wall and divides into **zygomaticotemporal** and **zygomaticofacial branches**:

- The **zygomaticotemporal branch** continues forward at the base of the lateral orbital wall, passes through a small bony canal in the zygomatic bone to enter the temporal fossa through a small foramen in the lateral orbital margin on the posterior surface of the frontal process of the zygomatic bone, and passes superficially to supply skin over the temple.
- The **zygomaticofacial branch** also passes forward at the base of the lateral orbital wall and leaves through a small bony canal, in the orbital margin, which opens via multiple small foramina on the anterolateral surface of the zygomatic bone, and its branches supply the adjacent skin.

### Posterior Superior Alveolar Nerve

The **posterior superior alveolar nerve** (Fig. 8.152) originates from the maxillary nerve [V2] in the pterygopalatine fossa and passes laterally out of the fossa through the pterygomaxillary fissure to enter the infratemporal fossa. It continues laterally and inferiorly to enter the posterior surface of the maxilla through a small alveolar foramen approximately midway between the last molar tooth and the inferior orbital fissure. It then passes inferiorly just deep to the mucosa of the maxillary sinus to join the superior dental plexus.

The posterior superior alveolar nerve supplies the molar teeth and adjacent buccal gingivae, and contributes to the supply of the maxillary sinus.

### Infra-orbital Nerve

The **infra-orbital nerve** (Fig. 8.152) is the anterior continuation of the maxillary nerve [V2] that leaves the pterygopalatine fossa through the inferior orbital fissure. It lies first in the infra-orbital groove in the floor of the orbit and then continues forward in the infra-orbital canal.

While in the infra-orbital groove and canal, the infra-orbital nerve gives origin to **middle** and **anterior superior alveolar nerves**, respectively, which ultimately join the superior alveolar plexus to supply the upper teeth:

- The **middle superior alveolar nerve** also supplies the maxillary sinus.
- The **anterior superior alveolar nerve** also gives origin to a small nasal branch, which passes medially through the lateral wall of the nasal cavity to supply parts of the areas of the nasal floor and walls.

The infra-orbital nerve exits the infra-orbital canal through the **infra-orbital foramen** inferior to the orbital margin and divides into **nasal**, **palpebral**, and **superior labial branches**:

- **Nasal branches** supply skin over the lateral aspect of the external nose and part of the nasal septum.
- **Palpebral branches** supply skin of the lower eyelid.
- **Superior labial branches** supply skin over the cheek and upper lip, and the related oral mucosa.

# Gray's Anatomy — Head & Neck：Section 34
## 神經翼管與翼顎神經節

---

## 神經翼管（Nerve of the Pterygoid Canal）與翼顎神經節（Pterygopalatine Ganglion）

神經翼管（圖 8.153）由以下兩條神經在顱中窩會合形成：

- **大岩神經（Greater Petrosal Nerve）**——顏面神經 [VII] 的分支
- **深岩神經（Deep Petrosal Nerve）**——內頸動脈叢的分支

神經翼管穿入翼顎窩（pterygopalatine fossa）並連接翼顎神經節，主要攜帶**節前副交感纖維**及**節後交感纖維**。

### 大岩神經（Greater Petrosal Nerve）

大岩神經起源於顏面神經 [VII] 的膝神經節（geniculate ganglion），位於顳骨內。大岩神經經由一個小管穿越顳骨，前表面經裂隙開口於顳骨岩部前方。它向前內側走行於顱中窩後緣，隨後於內頸動脈下方通過，抵達卵圓孔軟骨的上表面。

大岩神經於內頸動脈下方走行時，與深岩神經會合，形成神經翼管。

大岩神經攜帶副交感神經支配，分布於**口腔裂隙以上的所有腺體**，包括：

- **淚腺（Lacrimal Gland）**
- **鼻腔黏膜腺體**
- **口腔上半部的唾液腺**
- **眼眶內的淚腺**

大岩神經亦攜帶部分味覺（SA）纖維，經由小腭神經（lesser palatine nerve）來自軟腭。

> **臨床重點**：大岩神經攜帶副交感纖維至淚腺。任何影響此路徑的病變（從腦幹開始作為顏面神經 [VII] 的一部分，最終經眼神經 [V1] 的分支到達淚腺）皆可導致**乾眼症（dry eye）**，並可能最終導致受影響眼的視力喪失。

### 深岩神經（Deep Petrosal Nerve）

深岩神經由節後交感纖維組成，起源於頸部的**上頸神經節（Superior Cervical Sympathetic Ganglion）**，以內頸神經（internal carotid nerve）離開神經節。

在神經節中形成節前纖維的突觸來自 **T1 脊神經**。

內頸神經在內頸動脈穿過顱骨進入顱腔時，於內頸动脉周圍形成**內頸动脉叢（internal carotid plexus）**。部分叢纖維匯集形成深岩神經，於顱中窩離開內頸动脉叢，與顏面神經 [VII] 的大岩神經分支會合。

深岩神經攜帶**節後交感纖維**，主要分布於血管。

### 翼顎神經節（Pterygopalatine Ganglion）

神經翼管進入填充卵圓孔的軟骨上表面，向前穿過軟骨，進入蝶骨翼突根部之翼管（pterygoid canal）。神經穿過翼管後進入翼顎窩，與圍繞上頜神經 [V2] 分支形成的翼顎神經節會合（圖 8.153）。

翼顎神經節是頭部四個副交感神經節中最大者，由顏面神經 [VII] 經大岩神經及神經翼管攜帶之節前副交感纖維相關的**節後神經元細胞體**所構成。

源自翼顎神經節的節後副交感纖維，連同穿過神經節的節後交感纖維，加入上頜神經 [V2] 之神經節分支，形成**眼眶支、腭支、鼻支及咽支**，自神經節發出。

其他節後副交感及交感纖維向上穿過上頜神經 [V2] 之神經節分支，進入上頜神經主幹，隨顴神經、後上齒槽神經及眶下神經分布。其中，隨顴神經進入眼眶的節後副交感及交感纖維尤為重要，因為它們最終支配**淚腺**。

### 淚腺的神經支配

大約在眼眶壁中段，副交感及交感節後纖維離開顴顳分支（zygomaticotemporal branch of zygomatic nerve），形成一條特殊自主神經，沿眼眶外壁向上與**淚腺神經（lacrimal nerve）**會合（圖 8.153；亦見圖 8.84）。

淚腺神經是眼神經 [V1] 的主要一般感覺分支，於眼眶外壁與屋頂交界處向前走行。副交感及交感節後纖維隨淚腺神經到達淚腺。

---

## 上頜動脈（Maxillary Artery）

上頜動脈是頸部頸外動脈的主要分支，起源於下頜骨頸部附近，穿過顳下窩，經翼突上頜裂進入翼顎窩（圖 8.154）。

位於翼顎窩內的上頜動脈部分（第三段）位於翼顎神經節前方，發出分支，伴隨上頜神經 [V2] 及翼顎神經節的分支。

上頜動脈的分支包括：**後上齒槽動脈、眶下動脈、腭大動脈、咽动脉、蝶顎動脈及翼管動脈**（圖 8.154）。這些分支共同供應鼻腔大部分、鼻口腔頂、上下牙齒，並對鼻竇、口咽及眼眶底部的血液供應有所貢獻。

### 分支

#### 後上齒槽動脈（Posterior Superior Alveolar Artery）

後上齒槽動脈（圖 8.154）起源於上頜動脈穿過翼突上頜裂時。它與後上齒槽神經會合，經上頜骨顳下面之齒槽孔穿過，供應**臼齒及前臼齒、相鄰牙齦及上頜竇**。

#### 眶下動脈（Infra-orbital Artery）

眶下動脈（圖 8.154）向前與眶下神經伴行，經眶下裂離開翼顎窩。它隨眶下神經走行於眶下溝及眶下管，從眶下孔穿出供應面部結構。

眶下動脈在眶下管內發出：

- **眼眶底結構的血液供應分支**——下直肌、下斜肌及淚囊
- **前上齒槽動脈（Anterior Superior Alveolar Arteries）**（圖 8.154）——供應門齒、犬齒及上頜竇

#### 腭大動脈（Greater Palatine Artery）

腭大動脈（圖 8.154）向下隨腭神經經腭管走行。它發出**腭小分支**（圖 8.154），穿過腭小孔供應軟腭；隨後繼續經腭大孔供應硬腭。此血管於腭下表面向前走行進入切牙窩，向上經切牙管穿過，供應鼻腔 septal wall 的前部。

#### 咽支（Pharyngeal Branch）

上頜動脈的咽支（圖 8.154）向後走行，隨咽神經經腭咽管離開翼顎窩，供應鼻腔頂後部、蝶竇及**咽鼓管（Pharyngotympanic Tube）**。

#### 蝶顎動脈（Sphenopalatine Artery）

蝶顎動脈（圖 8.154）是上頜動脈的終支。它從翼顎窩內側經蝶顎孔離開，隨鼻神經走行，發出：

- **後外側鼻動脈（Posterior Lateral Nasal Arteries）**——供應鼻腔外側壁並對鼻竇供應有所貢獻
- **後隔分支（Posterior Septal Branches）**——向內跨過頂部供應鼻中隔——其中最大分支向前沿中隔向下，與腭大動脈末端吻合

#### 翼管動脈（Artery of the Pterygoid Canal）

翼管動脈向後進入翼管，供應周圍組織，經卵圓孔軟骨向下穿過後，終於**鼻咽黏膜**。

### 靜脈

由上頜動脈終部分支所供應區域的靜脈，通常隨這些分支返回翼顎窩。

靜脈在翼顎窩內匯集後，向外穿過翼突上頜裂，加入顳下窩的**翼狀靜脈叢（Pterygoid Plexus of Veins）**（圖 8.155）。

眶下靜脈引流眼眶下方，可經眶下裂外側直接進入顳下窩，繞過翼顎窩。

---

## 頸部（Neck）

頸部是連接頭部與軀幹的管道。前方從下頜骨下緣延伸至胸骨柄上表面，後方從枕骨上項線（superior nuchal line）延伸至 CVII 與 TI 椎體之間的椎間盤（圖 8.156）。

頸部管內有四個縱向排列的**筋膜隔室（Compartments）**：

- **內臟隔室（Visceral Compartment）**位於前方，包含部分消化及呼吸系統，以及數個內分泌腺體
- **脊椎隔室（Vertebral Compartment）**位於後方，包含頸椎、脊髓、頸神經及與脊柱相關的肌肉
- **兩個血管隔室（Vascular Compartments）**位於兩側，包含主要血管及**迷走神經 [X]**

所有隔室皆包含於獨特的**頸筋膜（Cervical Fascia）**層中。

### 頸部的分區

為描述方便，頸部分為**前三角（Anterior Triangle）**及**後三角（Posterior Triangle）**（圖 8.157）：

- **前三角**邊界：胸鎖乳突肌前緣、下頜骨下緣及頸部正中線
- **後三角**邊界：胸鎖乳突肌後緣、斜方肌前緣及鎖骨中外三分之一

---

## 頸部筋膜（Fascia of the Neck）

頸部筋膜具有多項獨特特徵。頸部淺層筋膜含有一層薄肌肉（**闊頸肌 Platysma**），它起於胸部淺層筋膜，向上附著於下頜骨並與面部肌肉交織，由顏面神經 [VII] 的頸支支配，僅見於此位置。

淺層筋膜深面，深層頸筋膜分為數層（圖 8.156）：

- **外被層（Investing Layer）**——圍繞頸部所有結構
- **椎前層（Prevertebral Layer）**——圍繞脊柱及其相關深層肌肉
- **氣管前層（Pretracheal Layer）**——包繞頸部內臟
- **頸動脈鞘（Carotid Sheaths）**——由其他三層筋膜參與構成，圍繞頸部兩側的主要神經血管束

### 外被層（Investing Layer）

外被層完全圍繞頸部（圖 8.158）。其後方附著於項韌帶及 CVII 椎體棘突，向前分叉包繞斜方肌，再融合為單層形成後三角頂部，再次分叉包繞胸鎖乳突肌，然後再次融合與對側會合。

前方，外被筋膜圍繞**舌骨下肌（Infrahyoid Muscles）**。

外被筋膜附著於：

- **上方**——外枕粗隆及上項線
- **外側**——乳突及顴弓
- **下方**——肩胛棘、肩峰、鎖骨及胸骨柄

頸外及頸前靜脈，以及頸神經叢的分支（小枕神經、耳大神經、頸橫神經及鎖骨上神經）皆穿過外被筋膜。

### 椎前層（Prevertebral Layer）

椎前層是包繞脊柱及其相關肌肉的圓柱狀筋膜層（圖 8.158）。此群肌肉包括椎前肌、前斜角肌、中斜角肌、後斜角肌及背部深層肌肉。

椎前筋膜後方沿項韌帶全长附著，上方形成一條連續環形線，附著於顱底。此環形線始於：

- **前方**——筋膜附著於枕骨底部、頸靜脈孔區及頸動脈管
- **外側**——附著於乳突
- **後方**——沿上項線終於外枕粗隆，與對側相連

前方，椎前筋膜附著於 CI 至 CVII 椎體橫突及椎體的前表面。

椎前筋膜在橫突附著點之間有一特殊結構。在此位置，椎前筋膜分為兩層，形成一個含疏鬆結締組織的縱向筋膜間隙，從顱底延伸穿過胸部（圖 8.158 及 8.159）。

椎前層在頸部下區還有一項特殊化。位於前外側的椎前筋膜從前、中斜角肌延伸，包繞臂神經叢及鎖骨下動靜脈進入腋窩，此筋膜延伸即為**腋鞘（Axillary Sheath）**。

### 氣管前層（Pretracheal Layer）

氣管前層由圍繞氣管、食道及甲狀腺的筋膜集合組成（圖 8.158）。前方包含一條跨頸部的氣管前筋膜，位於舌骨下肌後方，覆蓋氣管及甲狀腺。氣管前筋膜上方始於舌骨，下方於上胸腔與外界相通。外側，此筋膜包繞甲狀腺，更後方與圍繞食道的筋膜延續。

咽後方，氣管前層稱為**頰咽筋膜（Buccopharyngeal Fascia）**，將咽與椎前層分開（圖 8.159）。頰咽筋膜上方始於顱底，與覆蓋食道的筋膜融合後向下延伸入胸腔。

### 頸動脈鞘（Carotid Sheath）

每側頸動脈鞘是包繞**總頸動脈、頸內動脈、頸內靜脈及迷走神經**的筋膜柱（圖 8.158），這些結構穿過頸部。它由外被層、椎前層及氣管前層共同構成，但各層參與程度各異。

---

## 筋膜隔室（Fascial Compartments）

各層頸筋膜的排列將頸部分為四個縱向隔室（圖 8.156）：

- **第一隔室**最大，包含其他三層，為外被層所圍繞的區域
- **第二隔室**由脊柱及其相關深層肌肉組成，位於椎前層之內
- **第三隔室（內臟隔室）**包含咽、氣管、食道及甲狀腺，由氣管前層包繞
- **第四隔室（頸動脈鞘）**由從顱底至胸腔的神經血管結構及其周圍筋膜鞘組成

---

## 筋膜間隙（Fascial Spaces）

頸部各筋膜層之間存在間隙，可能為感染從頸部扩散至縱膈的途徑。

可能涉及的間隙有三個（圖 8.159）：

- **氣管前間隙（Pretracheal Space）**——位於頸筋膜外被層（覆蓋舌骨下肌後面）與氣管前筋膜（覆蓋氣管、甲狀腺前面）之間，向下延伸至頸部與上縱膈前部
- **咽後間隙（Retropharyngeal Space）**——位於頰咽筋膜（覆蓋咽及食道後面）與椎前筋膜（覆蓋頸椎橫突及椎體前面）之間，從顱底延伸至上縱膈後部
- **椎前層內的間隙**——覆蓋頸椎橫突及椎體前面的椎前層分為兩板，形成一個從顱底延伸穿過後縱膈至橫膈的筋膜間隙

---

## 淺層靜脈引流（Superficial Venous Drainage）

頸部淺層靜脈引流的主要通道是**頸外靜脈（External Jugular Vein）**及**頸前靜脈（Anterior Jugular Veins）**（圖 8.160）。

### 頸外靜脈（External Jugular Veins）

頸外靜脈於下頜骨角後方，由**耳後靜脈（Posterior Auricular Vein）**及**下頜後靜脈（Retromandibular Vein）**會合形成：

- **耳後靜脈**引流耳後及上方之头皮血液
- **下頌後靜脈**由顳淺靜脈及上頜靜脈於腮腺內合併形成，向下至下頜骨角，分為前支及後支——後支與耳後靜脈合併形成頸外靜脈，前支與面靜脈合併形成**面總靜脈（Common Facial Vein）**，向深部走行，成為頸內靜脈的支流

頸外靜脈形成後，於淺層筋膜中垂直下行於頸部，全程位於**胸鎖乳突肌（sternocleidomastoid muscle）**淺層，斜行穿越該肌下行。

頸外靜脈抵達頸下部、鎖骨上方、胸鎖乳突肌後方時，穿透頸筋膜外被層，經鎖骨深部進入**鎖骨下靜脈（Subclavian Vein）**。

頸外靜脈沿途接收的支流包括：**頸後外靜脈（Posterior External Jugular Vein）**（引流頸後淺層）及**頸橫靜脈及肩胛上靜脈（Transverse Cervical and Suprascapular Veins）**（引流肩胛後區）。

### 頸前靜脈（Anterior Jugular Veins）

頸前靜脈雖有變異且不恆定，但通常描述為引流頸部前面的靜脈（圖 8.160）。這對靜脈通道較細小，於舌骨上方或附近匯合。形成後，每條頸前靜脈沿頸部正中線兩側下行。

下方，靠近胸鎖乳突肌內側附著處，每條頸前靜脈穿透頸筋膜外被層進入鎖骨下靜脈。有時頸前靜脈可在頸外靜脈進入鎖骨下靜脈之前直接注入頸外靜脈。

通常，左右頸前靜脈於胸骨上凹（suprasternal notch）區域相互交通，由**頸靜脈弓（Jugular Venous Arch）**連接。

---

## 臨床重點：頭頸部筋膜平面

頸部含有一系列被緊密筋膜包繞的隔室，所有這些隔室皆位於頸筋膜外被層之內。

從臨床角度而言，這些隔室的重要性在於：**感染傾向於在隔室內或在各筋膜層之間的間隙中扩散**。例如，若感染發生於氣管前間隙，可能向下扩散至下縱膈，位於心包前方。

> **臨床重點**：頭頸部感染可沿筋膜平面扩散至縱膈，臨床上需警惕此途徑。

---

## 臨床重點：中心靜脈通路

在大多數情況下，手臂及腿部的周邊靜脈通路足以用於給予靜脈藥物及液體，以及取得血液进行分析。在某些情況下，需要在上頸靜脈或鎖骨下靜脈放置較大口徑導管，例如：血液透析、腸道外營養或給予容易引起靜脈炎的藥物。

過去，頸部或鎖骨下靜脈的「盲目穿刺」是建立中心靜脈通路的標準做法。然而，鎖骨下靜脈穿刺並非沒有併發症。鎖骨下靜脈向下走行時，位於鎖骨後方，它經過肺尖上方。針穿刺失誤可能刺穿或穿過此結構，刺破頂層胸膜，導致**氣胸（Pneumothorax）**。意外的动脉穿刺及靜脈撕裂也可能產生**血氣胸（Hemopneumothorax）**。

頸內靜脈穿刺（圖 8.161）的併發症較少，但仍需注意局部血腫及頸總動脈損傷。

目前的實踐做法是使用**超聲波（Ultrasound）**定位主要血管，並在直接視野下建立中心靜脈通路，以避免任何重大併發症。

# Head & Neck — Segment 35
## Anterior Triangle of the Neck（頸前三角區）

---

## Overview（概述）

The **anterior triangle of the neck** is outlined by:

- **Sternocleidomastoid muscle** laterally（外側）
- **Inferior border of the mandible** superiorly（上側）
- **Midline of the neck** medially（內側）

> **臨床重點**：Anterior triangle 是頸部重要的解剖分區，其邊界定義了臨床上多個手術入路與結構定位的參考平面。

### Subdivisions of the Anterior Triangle（頸前三角區的細分）

| Subdivision | Superior border | Inferior border | Lateral/Medial border |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Submandibular triangle**（下頜下三角）| Mandible | Anterior & posterior bellies of **digastric muscle** | — |
| **Submental triangle**（頦下三角）| — | **Hyoid bone** | Anterior belly of **digastric** / Midline |
| **Muscular triangle**（肌性三角）| **Hyoid bone** | **Superior belly of omohyoid** / **Sternocleidomastoid** | Midline |
| **Carotid triangle**（頸动脉三角）| **Stylohyoid muscle** / posterior belly of **digastric** | **Superior belly of omohyoid** | **Sternocleidomastoid** |

Each triangle contains structures that may be:
- Identified within a specific triangle
- Passing into a triangle from outside
- Originating in one triangle and passing to another
- Passing through several triangles

---

## Muscles of the Anterior Triangle（頸前三角區的肌肉）

### Grouped by Position Relative to the Hyoid Bone

- **Suprahyoid muscles**（舌骨上肌）：superior to the **hyoid bone**
- **Infrahyoid muscles**（舌骨下肌）：inferior to the **hyoid bone**

---

## Suprahyoid Muscles（舌骨上肌）

> **臨床重點**：Suprahyoid muscles 在吞嚥時提升 **hyoid bone**，為臨床上評估吞嚥功能的重要參考。

### Muscles Overview（總覽）

| Muscle | Origin | Insertion | Innervation | Function |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Stylohyoid** | Base of **styloid process** | Lateral area of body of **hyoid bone** | **Facial nerve [VII]** | Pulls **hyoid bone** posterosuperiorly |
| **Digastric**（二腹肌）| Anterior belly: **digastric fossa** of mandible；Posterior belly: **mastoid notch** of temporal bone | Tendon attaches to body of **hyoid bone** | Anterior belly: **mylohyoid nerve [V₃]**；Posterior belly: **Facial nerve [VII]** | Opens mouth（降下頜）/ Raises **hyoid bone** |
| **Mylohyoid**（頦舌骨肌）| **Mylohyoid line** on mandible | Body of **hyoid bone** & fibers from opposite side | **Mylohyoid nerve [V₃]** | Supports & elevates floor of mouth；Elevates **hyoid bone** |
| **Geniohyoid**（頦舌肌）| **Inferior mental spine** of mandible | Anterior surface of body of **hyoid bone** | Branch from **C1** along **hypoglossal nerve [XII]** | Fixed mandible → elevates & pulls **hyoid** forward；Fixed **hyoid** → pulls mandible downward |

---

### Stylohyoid Muscle（莖突舌骨肌）

The **stylohyoid muscle** arises from the base of the **styloid process** and passes anteroinferiorly to attach to the lateral area of the body of the **hyoid bone**. During swallowing it pulls the **hyoid bone** posterosuperiorly and is innervated by the **facial nerve [VII]**.

---

### Digastric Muscle（二腹肌）

The **digastric muscle** has two bellies connected by a tendon, which attaches to the body of the **hyoid bone**:

- **Posterior belly**: arises from the **mastoid notch** on the medial side of the **mastoid process** of the **temporal bone**
- **Anterior belly**: arises from the **digastric fossa** on the lower inside of the **mandible**

The tendon between the two bellies attached to the **hyoid bone** is the point of insertion of both bellies. Because of this arrangement, the muscle has multiple actions depending on which bone is fixed:

- When the **mandible** is fixed → raises the **hyoid bone**
- When the **hyoid bone** is fixed → opens the mouth by lowering the **mandible**

> **臨床重點**：Digastric muscle 的雙重神經支配（前腹：**mylohyoid nerve [V₃]**；後腹：**facial nerve [VII]**）在臨床上用於鑑別顱內與顱外病灶具有參考價值。

---

### Mylohyoid Muscle（頦舌骨肌）

The **mylohyoid muscle** is superior to the anterior belly of the **digastric** and, with its partner from the opposite side, forms the floor of the mouth. It originates from the **mylohyoid line** on the medial surface of the body of the **mandible** and inserts into the **hyoid bone**, also blending with the **mylohyoid muscle** from the opposite side.

This muscle supports and elevates the floor of the mouth and elevates the **hyoid bone**. It is innervated by the **mandibular division [V₃]** of the **trigeminal nerve [V]**.

---

### Geniohyoid Muscle（頦舌肌）

The **geniohyoid muscle** is superior to the floor of the oral cavity and is not generally considered a muscle of the anterior triangle of the neck; however, it can be regarded as a suprahyoid muscle. A narrow muscle, it is superior to the medial part of each **mylohyoid muscle**. The muscles from each side are next to each other in the midline.

The **geniohyoid** arises from the **inferior mental spine** of the **mandible** and passes backward and downward to insert on the body of the **hyoid bone**.

It has two functions depending on which bone is fixed:

- Fixation of the **mandible** → elevates and pulls the **hyoid bone** forward
- Fixation of the **hyoid bone** → pulls the **mandible** downward and inward

The **geniohyoid** is innervated by a branch from the **anterior ramus of C1** carried along the **hypoglossal nerve [XII]**.

---

## Infrahyoid Muscles（舌骨下肌）

The four **infrahyoid muscles** are related to the muscular triangle. They attach the **hyoid bone** to inferior structures and depress the **hyoid bone**. They also provide a stable point of attachment for the suprahyoid muscles. Because of their appearance, they are sometimes referred to as the **"strap muscles"**（帶狀肌）.

| Muscle | Origin | Insertion | Innervation | Function |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Sternohyoid**（胸骨舌骨肌）| Posterior aspect of **sternoclavicular joint** and adjacent **manubrium of sternum** | Body of **hyoid bone** medial to attachment of **omohyoid** | **Anterior rami of C1–C3** via **ansa cervicalis** | Depresses **hyoid bone** after swallowing |
| **Omohyoid**（肩胛舌骨肌）| Superior border of **scapula** medial to **suprascapular notch** | Lower border of body of **hyoid bone** just lateral to attachment of **sternohyoid** | **Anterior rami of C1–C3** via **ansa cervicalis** | Depresses and fixes **hyoid bone** |
| **Thyrohyoid**（甲狀舌骨肌）| Oblique line on lamina of **thyroid cartilage** | Greater horn and adjacent aspect of body of **hyoid bone** | **C1** via **hypoglossal nerve [XII]** | Depresses **hyoid**；Fixed **hyoid** → raises larynx |
| **Sternothyroid**（胸骨甲狀肌）| Posterior surface of **manubrium of sternum** | Oblique line on lamina of **thyroid cartilage** | **Anterior rami of C1–C3** via **ansa cervicalis** | Draws larynx (**thyroid cartilage**) downward |

---

### Sternohyoid Muscle（胸骨舌骨肌）

The **sternohyoid muscle** is a long, thin muscle originating from the posterior aspect of the **sternoclavicular joint** and adjacent **manubrium of the sternum**. It ascends to insert onto the body of the **hyoid bone**. It depresses the **hyoid bone** and is innervated by the **anterior rami of C1 to C3** through the **ansa cervicalis**.

---

### Omohyoid Muscle（肩胛舌骨肌）

Lateral to the **sternohyoid muscle** is the **omohyoid muscle**. This muscle consists of two bellies with an intermediate tendon in both the posterior and anterior triangles of the neck:

- **Inferior belly**: begins on the **superior border of the scapula**, medial to the **suprascapular notch**, and passes forward and upward across the posterior triangle ending at the intermediate tendon
- **Superior belly**: begins at the intermediate tendon and ascends to attach to the body of the **hyoid bone** just lateral to the attachment of the **sternohyoid**
- **Intermediate tendon**: attached to the **clavicle**, near its medial end, by a fascial sling

The **omohyoid** depresses and fixes the **hyoid bone**. It is innervated by the **anterior rami of C1 to C3** through the **ansa cervicalis**.

---

### Thyrohyoid Muscle（甲狀舌骨肌）

The **thyrohyoid muscle** is deep to the superior parts of the **omohyoid** and **sternohyoid**. Originating at the **oblique line on the lamina of the thyroid cartilage**, it passes upward to insert into the **greater horn** and adjacent aspect of the body of the **hyoid bone**.

The **thyrohyoid muscle** has variable functions depending on which bone is fixed. Generally, it depresses the **hyoid**, but when the **hyoid** is fixed it raises the larynx (e.g., when high notes are sung). It is innervated by fibers from the **anterior ramus of C1** that travel with the **hypoglossal nerve [XII]**.

> **臨床重點**：Thyrohyoid muscle 在發高音時協助提升喉部，此功能常用於評估聲帶運動功能。

---

### Sternothyroid Muscle（胸骨甲狀肌）

Lying beneath the **sternohyoid** and in continuity with the **thyrohyoid**, the **sternothyroid** is the last muscle in the infrahyoid group. It arises from the **posterior surface of the manubrium of the sternum** and passes upward to attach to the **oblique line on the lamina of the thyroid cartilage**.

The **sternothyroid muscle** draws the larynx (**thyroid cartilage**) downward and is innervated by the **anterior rami of C1 to C3** through the **ansa cervicalis**.

---

## Vessels（血管）

Passing through the anterior triangle of the neck are the **common carotid arteries** and their branches, the **external** and **internal carotid arteries**. These vessels supply all structures of the head and neck. Associated with this arterial system are the **internal jugular vein** and its tributaries.

---

### Carotid System（頸动脉系統）

#### Common Carotid Arteries（總頸动脉）

The **common carotid arteries** are the beginning of the carotid system:

- **Right common carotid artery**: originates from the **brachiocephalic trunk** immediately posterior to the right **sternoclavicular joint**; entirely in the neck throughout its course
- **Left common carotid artery**: begins in the thorax as a direct branch of the **arch of the aorta** and passes superiorly to enter the neck near the left **sternoclavicular joint**

Both right and left **common carotid arteries** ascend through the neck, just lateral to the **trachea** and **esophagus**, within a fascial compartment (the **carotid sheath**). They give off **no branches** as they pass through the neck.

Near the superior edge of the **thyroid cartilage** each common carotid artery divides into its two terminal branches — the **external** and **internal carotid arteries**.

> **臨床重點**：Carotid sheath（頸动脉鞘）是頸部重要的筋膜解剖標的，臨床上頸總动脉分歧部（bifurcation）是定位頸动脉斑塊與評估頸动脉狹窄的關鍵位置。

#### Carotid Bifurcation（頸动脉分歧部）

At the bifurcation, the **common carotid artery** and the beginning of the **internal carotid artery** are dilated. This dilation is the **carotid sinus**（頸动脉竇）and contains receptors that monitor changes in blood pressure; it is innervated by a branch of the **glossopharyngeal nerve [IX]**.

Another accumulation of receptors in the area of the bifurcation is responsible for detecting changes in blood chemistry, primarily oxygen content. This is the **carotid body**（頸动脉體）and is innervated by branches from both the **glossopharyngeal [IX]** and **vagus [X]** nerves.

> **臨床重點**：Carotid sinus 含有壓力感受器，其過度刺激可能引起**頸动脉竇性暈厥**（carotid sinus syncope）；Carotid body 為化學感受器，與缺氧監測相關。

#### Internal Carotid Arteries（內頸动脉）

After its origin, the **internal carotid artery** ascends toward the base of the skull. It gives off **no branches in the neck** and enters the cranial cavity through the **carotid canal** in the **petrous part of the temporal bone**.

The **internal carotid arteries** supply the cerebral hemispheres, the eyes and the contents of the orbits, and the forehead.

#### External Carotid Arteries（外頸动脉）

The **external carotid arteries** begin giving off branches immediately after the bifurcation of the common carotid arteries:

| Branch | Supplies |
|---|---|
| **Superior thyroid artery**（甲狀腺上动脉）| **Thyrohyoid muscle**, internal structures of larynx, **sternocleidomastoid** and **cricothyroid muscles**, **thyroid gland** |
| **Ascending pharyngeal artery**（咽升动脉）| **Pharyngeal constrictors** and **stylopharyngeus**, palate, tonsil, **pharyngotympanic tube**, meninges in posterior cranial fossa |
| **Lingual artery**（舌动脉）| Muscles of tongue, **palatine tonsil**, soft palate, epiglottis, floor of mouth, **sublingual gland** |
| **Facial artery**（面动脉）| All structures in face from inferior border of mandible to medial corner of eye, soft palate, tonsil, **pharyngotympanic tube**, **submandibular gland** |
| **Occipital artery**（枕动脉）| **Sternocleidomastoid**, meninges in posterior cranial fossa, mastoid cells, deep muscles of back, posterior scalp |
| **Posterior auricular artery**（耳後动脉）| **Parotid gland**, nearby muscles, external ear and scalp posterior to ear, middle and inner ear structures |
| **Superficial temporal artery**（顳淺动脉）| **Parotid gland** and duct, **masseter**, lateral face, anterior part of external ear, **temporalis**, parietal and temporal fossae |
| **Maxillary artery**（上頜动脉）| **External acoustic meatus**, **tympanic membrane**, **temporomandibular joint**, dura mater, **trigeminal ganglion**, **mylohyoid**, mandibular teeth, **temporalis**, skull bones in temporal fossa, structures in **infratemporal fossa**, **maxillary sinus**, upper teeth and gingivae, infra-orbital skin, palate, roof of pharynx, nasal cavity |

---

#### Superficial Temporal Artery & Maxillary Artery（顳淺动脉與上頜动脉）

- **Superficial temporal artery**: one of the terminal branches — beginning posterior to the **neck of the mandible**, passes anterior to the ear, crosses the **zygomatic process of the temporal bone**, and above this point divides into anterior and posterior branches
- **Maxillary artery**: the larger of the two terminal branches — arising posterior to the **neck of the mandible**, passes through the **parotid gland**, continues medial to the **neck of the mandible** and into the **infratemporal fossa**, and continues through this area into the **pterygopalatine fossa**

---

### Veins（靜脈）

Collecting blood from the skull, brain, superficial face, and parts of the neck, the **internal jugular vein** begins as a dilated continuation of the **sigmoid sinus**, which is a dural venous sinus. This initial dilated part is referred to as the **superior bulb of jugular vein** and receives another dural venous sinus (the **inferior petrosal sinus**) soon after it is formed. It exits the skull through the **jugular foramen** associated with the **glossopharyngeal [IX]**, **vagus [X]**, and **accessory [XI]** nerves, and enters the **carotid sheath**.

The **internal jugular vein** traverses the neck within the **carotid sheath**, initially posterior to the **internal carotid artery**, but passes to a more lateral position farther down. It remains lateral to the **common carotid artery** through the rest of the neck with the **vagus nerve [X]** posterior and partially between the two vessels.

The paired **internal jugular veins** join with the **subclavian veins** posterior to the sternal end of the **clavicle** to form the right and left **brachiocephalic veins**.

Tributaries to each **internal jugular vein** include the **inferior petrosal sinus**, and the **facial**, **lingual**, **pharyngeal**, **occipital**, **superior thyroid**, and **middle thyroid veins**.

> **臨床重點**：Internal jugular vein 在頸部中央靜脈導管放置與靜脈營養輸注中具有重要臨床意義，其位置變異可能影響手術入路規劃。

---

#### Jugular Venous Pulse（頸靜脈搏動）

> **臨床重點**：**Jugular venous pulse (JVP)** 是評估靜脈壓力與波形的重要臨床徵象，反映右心功能。正常 JVP 波形消失或異常可能提示心臟功能障礙。

---

## Nerves（神經）

Numerous **cranial** and **peripheral nerves**:

- pass through the anterior triangle of the neck as they continue to their final destination
- send branches to structures in or forming boundaries of the anterior triangle of the neck
- while in the anterior triangle of the neck, send branches to nearby structures

**Cranial nerves** in these categories include the **facial [VII]**, **glossopharyngeal [IX]**, **vagus [X]**, **accessory [XI]**, and **hypoglossal [XII]**.

**Branches of spinal nerves** include the **transverse cervical nerve** from the **cervical plexus** and the upper and lower roots of the **ansa cervicalis**.

---

### Facial Nerve [VII]（顏面神經）

After emerging from the **stylomastoid foramen**, the **facial nerve [VII]** gives off branches that innervate two muscles associated with the anterior triangle of the neck:

- the **posterior belly of the digastric**
- the **stylohyoid**

The **facial nerve [VII]** also innervates the **platysma muscle** that overlies the anterior triangle and part of the posterior triangle of the neck.

---

*Source: Gray's Anatomy for Students — Head & Neck, Segment 35*

# Gray's Anatomy — Head & Neck：Section 36
## 顱神經與頸部區域解剖（續）

---

## 舌下神經 [XII]

舌下神經 [XII] 離開顱腔後，經由舌下神經管下行，顱外段最初位於**內頸靜脈**與**頸內動脈**的內側。隨著神經繼續下降，它會從兩者之間向外穿出，隨即向前彎繞過**枕動脈**，跨越**頸內動脈**、**頸外動脈**及**舌動脈**的外側面，隨後走行於**二腹肌後腹**與**莖突舌骨肌**的深層，最後越過**舌骨舌肌**表面，消失於**下頜舌骨肌**深層。

舌下神經 [XII] 支配舌頭，但在通過頸部前三角時不發出任何分支。

### 圖 8.171：舌下神經 [XII]

> **術語對照**
> - Posterior belly of digastric muscle → **二腹肌後腹**
> - Superior thyroid artery → **甲狀腺上動脈**
> - Internal carotid artery → **頸內動脈**
> - Internal jugular vein → **內頸靜脈**
> - Sternocleidomastoid branch → **胸鎖乳突肌支**
> - Occipital artery → **枕動脈**
> - Sternocleidomastoid muscle → **胸鎖乳突肌**
> - Trapezius muscle → **斜方肌**
> - Stylohyoid muscle → **莖突舌骨肌**
> - Hyoglossus muscle → **舌骨舌肌**

---

## 橫頸神經（Transverse Cervical Nerve）

橫頸神經是**頸神經叢**的分支，起源於 **C2 與 C3** 前支。它從**胸鎖乳突肌**後緣中點附近離開肌肉深層，繞過該肌前緣後以橫向方式跨過頸部前方，支配頸部的皮節感覺。

---

## 頸袢（Ansa Cervicalis）

頸袢是由 **C1 至 C3** 組成的神經纖維環，支配頸部前三角中的「帶狀肌」（strap muscles）。

### 組成與分支

- **上根（Superior Root）**：來自 **C1** 的神經纖維在舌下神經 [XII] 離開顱腔後隨即加入其中。當舌下神經 [XII] 下降完畢並開始向前跨越頸內、外頸動脈時，部分來自 C1 的纖維離開舌下神經，在內頸靜脈與頸內頸動脈之間下行，支配**肩胛舌骨肌上腹**、**胸骨舌骨肌**上段及**胸骨甲狀肌**上段。

- **下根（Inferior Root）**：直接來自**頸神經叢**（含 **C2 與 C3** 纖維），在內頸靜脈內側或外側下行後轉向內側與上根會合，形成頸袢環。頸袢隨後發出分支支配**肩胛舌骨肌下腹**、**胸骨舌骨肌**下段及**胸骨甲狀肌**下段。

### 圖 8.172：橫頸神經

### 圖 8.173：頸袢

---

## 消化系統與呼吸系統元素

食道、氣管、咽與喉均位於頸部，並與頸部前三角相鄰。

### 食道（Esophagus）

食道為胃腸道的一部分，在頸部下段僅有很短的路徑。它始於**第六頸椎（CVI）**平面，向上與咽相連，向下穿過胸廓入口進入胸腔。食道直接位於**椎柱**前方。

### 氣管（Trachea）

氣管為下呼吸道的一部分，與食道相同，起始於 **CVI** 平面，向上與喉相延續。氣管直接位於食道前方，在正中線上向下進入胸腔。

### 咽（Pharynx）與喉（Larynx）

咽是空氣與食物的共同通道，連接頭部的呼吸與消化區室與頸部下段的相應區室。

喉是下呼吸道的上端，向下與氣管延續，向後上方與咽相連。

---

## 甲狀腺與副甲狀腺

甲狀腺與副甲狀腺均為內分泌腺，位於頸部前方的位置。兩者皆由咽壁外突形成，並在發育過程中向下遷移至最終位置。

**甲狀腺**為一較大的不成對腺體；**副甲狀腺**通常有四枚，體積小，位於甲狀腺的後表面。

---

## 甲狀腺（Thyroid Gland）

### 形態與位置

甲狀腺位於頸部前方，甲狀軟骨下方及外側（圖 8.174）。由兩個側葉組成（覆蓋氣管、甲狀軟骨下半部及環狀軟骨的前外側面），左右側葉由**峽部（isthmus）**連接，跨越第二與第三氣管軟骨的前表面。

甲狀腺位於**胸骨舌骨肌**、**胸骨甲狀肌**與**肩胛舌骨肌**的深層，屬於頸部內臟區室。該區室亦包含**咽**、**氣管**與**食道**，並由**氣管前筋膜（pretracheal fascia）**包圍。

### 胚胎髮育

甲狀腺起源於咽底部靠近舌根部的正中外突。**舌盲孔（foramen cecum）**即代表其起源位置，**甲狀舌管（thyroglossal duct）**則標誌著甲狀腺遷移至成人最終位置的路徑。甲狀舌管通常在發育早期消失，但可能殘留為囊腫或與盲孔相連（瘻管）。

### 異位甲狀腺組織

甲狀腺組織可能出現於：

- **舌部**（舌甲狀腺，lingual thyroid）
- **遷移路徑上的任何位置**
- **甲狀腺上方沿甲狀舌管延伸處**（錐狀葉，pyramidal lobe）

### 圖 8.174：甲狀腺

> **臨床重點**：甲狀腺發育與甲狀舌管囊腫
> 甲狀腺起源於舌根附近的小團組織，經由甲狀舌管從舌背側的**舌盲孔**下降，途經**舌骨中部前緣**附近，最終停於**氣管前緣**的頸根部。由於甲狀腺組織的遷移可能在發育過程中被阻斷，異位甲狀腺組織臨床上相對少見。更常見的是甲狀舌管囊性變，其典型症狀為**中線腫塊**。超聲波可輕易確定囊腫的性質與位置，治療方式為手術切除。手術時必須將整條管道及**舌骨前緣的一小部分**一併切除，以防復發。

---

## 甲狀腺的血管供應

甲狀腺由兩條主要動脈供血。

### 甲狀腺上動脈（Superior Thyroid Artery）

甲狀腺上動脈是**頸外動脈**的第一條分支。向下沿**甲狀舌骨肌**外側緣走行，到達甲狀腺側葉上極後分為前後腺支：

- **前腺支**：沿甲狀腺上緣走行，與對側同名分支在峽部前方吻合
- **後腺支**：走向甲狀腺後方，可能與**甲狀腺下動脈**吻合

### 甲狀腺下動脈（Inferior Thyroid Artery）

甲狀腺下動脈是**甲狀甲狀幹（thyrocervical trunk）**的分支，起源於**鎖骨下動脈**第一段。向上沿**前斜角肌**內側緣走行，經**頸鞘**後方，到達甲狀腺側葉下極。

甲狀腺下動脈在甲狀腺處分為：

- **下支**：供應甲狀腺下極，與甲狀腺上動脈後支吻合
- **上行支**：供應副甲狀腺

偶爾可見**甲狀腺最下動脈（thyroid ima artery）**起自**頭臂幹**或**主動脈弓**，上行於氣管前方，供應甲狀腺。

### 圖 8.175：甲狀腺血管（前面觀）

---

## 甲狀腺的靜脈與淋巴引流

甲狀腺由三條靜脈引流：

- **甲狀腺上靜脈**：主要引流甲狀腺上动脉供應區域
- **甲狀腺中靜脈**與**甲狀腺下靜脈**：引流甲狀腺其餘部分

甲狀腺上、中靜脈匯入**內頸靜脈**；甲狀腺下靜脈分別匯入左右**頭臂靜脈**。

甲狀腺的淋巴引流至**氣管旁淋巴結（paratracheal nodes）**及沿**內頸靜脈**、位於**肩胛舌骨肌**下方深層的**頸深淋巴結**。

### 圖 8.176，甲狀腺上、下動脈與左、右喉返神經（後面觀）

---

## 喉返神經（Recurrent Laryngeal Nerves）

甲狀腺與喉返神經關係密切。喉返神經自**迷走神經 [X]** 分出後，右侧繞過**鎖骨下動脈**，左侧繞過**主動脈弓**，而後在**氣管**與**食道**之間的溝槽中上行。它們走行於甲狀腺側葉後內側面深層，通過**咽下縮肌**下緣深層進入喉。

喉返神經與甲狀腺下动脉分支共同與甲狀腺兩側固定於氣管及環狀軟骨的韌帶關係密切，甲狀腺手術時需特別注意這些解剖關係。

---

## 副甲狀腺（Parathyroid Glands）

副甲狀腺為兩對小的卵圓形淡黃色結構，位於甲狀腺側葉的深層表面，分為**上副甲狀腺**與**下副甲狀腺**。然而其位置變異性很大，可能位於**頸總動脈分叉處**上方乃至**縱隔**下方。

副甲狀腺起源於**第三對**（下副甲狀腺）與**第四對**（上副甲狀腺）咽囊，在發育過程中遷移至成體位置，故依其起源位置命名。

副甲狀腺的供血動脈為**甲狀腺下动脉**，靜脈與淋巴引流與甲狀腺相同。

> **臨床重點**：副甲狀腺異位
> 副甲狀腺由第三與第四咽囊發育而來，在發育過程中遷移至成體位置。其位置可能高度變異，有時位於頸部高處或胸腔內。這些位置的腫瘤皆可能發生。

### 圖 8.177：甲狀腺切除術中可見左葉甲狀腺（甲狀腺腫）與喉返神經的密切關係

---

## 甲狀腺手術（Thyroidectomy）

甲狀腺切除術為常見外科手術，多數情況下涉及切除部分或大部分甲狀腺組織，通常用於良性疾病如**多結節性甲狀腺腫（multinodular goiter）**或**甲狀腺癌**。

由於甲狀腺的位置，手術過程中可能損傷**副甲狀腺**與**喉返神經**。甲狀腺手術前、後需評估聲帶功能，因為喉返神經與固定甲狀腺於喉的韌帶關係密切，極易在手術中受損。

---

## 甲狀腺疾病（Thyroid Gland Pathology）

甲狀腺疾病的評估需從兩方面著手：

1. **甲狀腺大小**：瀰漫性或局部性增大，有多種病因
2. **甲狀腺功能**：分泌過多或過少甲狀腺素（thyroxine）

### 多結節性甲狀腺腫（Multinodular Goiter）

為甲狀腺瀰漫性不規則增大，伴有甲狀腺組織增生與膠質囊腫形成。多數患者為**甲狀腺功能正常（euthyroid）**。典型症狀為頸部瀰漫性腫塊，可採藥物治療或若手塊過大影響生活或造成呼吸問題則需手術切除。

### 甲狀腺結節

甲狀腺內的單發結節可能是多結節性甲狀腺腫中的主結節，也可能是甲狀腺的獨立腫瘤。獨立腫瘤依其細胞型態可能分泌或不分泌甲狀腺素，通常以手術切除治療。

### 自體免疫疾病

自體免疫疾病可能刺激甲狀腺過度分泌甲狀腺素，並可能伴有甲狀腺外的表現，包括**突眼症（exophthalmos）**、**脛前黏液水腫（pretibial myxedema）**及**指甲變化**。甲狀腺也可因疾病而萎縮，導致甲狀腺素分泌不足（**黏液性水腫，myxedema**）。

---

## 前三角各分區內的結構定位

頸部前三角主要結構的區域位置摘要見表 8.14。

### 表 8.14：頸部前三角分區——區域定位法

| 分區 | 境界 | 內容物 |
|------|------|--------|
| **頦下三角（Submental triangle）**（不成對）| 下頜聯合；二腹肌前腹；舌骨體 | 頦下淋巴結；形成頸前靜脈的支流 |
| **下頜下三角（Submandibular triangle）**（成對）| 下頜骨下緣；二腹肌前腹；二腹肌後腹 | 下頜下腺；下頜下淋巴結；舌下神經 [XII]；下頜舌骨神經；面動、靜脈 |
| **頸動脈三角（Carotid triangle）**（成對）| 二腹肌後腹；肩胛舌骨肌上腹；胸鎖乳突肌前緣 | 面總靜脈支流；面神經 [VII] 頸支；頸總動脈；頸內、外頸動脈；甲狀腺上、咽升、舌、面、枕动脉；內頸靜脈；迷走神經 [X]、副神經 [XI]、舌下神經 [XII]；頸袢上、下根；橫頸神經 |
| **肌三角（Muscular triangle）**（成對）| 頸正中線；肩胛舌骨肌上腹；胸鎖乳突肌前緣 | 胸骨舌骨、肩胛舌骨、胸骨甲狀、甲狀舌骨肌；甲狀腺與副甲狀腺；咽 |

---

## 頸部後三角（Posterior Triangle of the Neck）

頸部後三角位於頸部外側，與上肢直接連續（圖 8.179）。其境界如下：

- **前方**：胸鎖乳突肌後緣
- **後方**：斜方肌前緣
- **基底部**：鎖骨外三分之一
- **頂部**：乳突後方的枕骨，此處斜方肌與胸鎖乳突肌的附著點會合

### 後三角的頂

後三角的頂為**頸筋膜的封套層**，在通過該區域時包繞胸鎖乳突肌與斜方肌。

### 後三角的底（肌層）

後三角的底覆有**椎前筋膜**，由上而下依次為：

- **頭夾肌（Splenius capitis）**
- **提肩胛肌（Levator scapulae）**
- **後、中、前斜角肌（Posterior, middle, and anterior scalene muscles）**

### 圖 8.179：頸部後三角的境界

> **重要結構**
> - Sternocleidomastoid muscle → **胸鎖乳突肌**
> - Superior belly of omohyoid → **肩胛舌骨肌上腹**
> - Inferior belly of omohyoid → **肩胛舌骨肌下腹**
> - Posterior triangle → **後三角**
> - Omoclavicular or subclavian triangle → **肩鎖或鎖骨下三角**

---

# Gray's Anatomy — Head & Neck
## Segment 37：Posterior Triangle of the Neck（頸後三角）

---

## 概述

**Posterior triangle of the neck（頸後三角）** 是頸部兩側的重要解剖區域，由 **sternocleidomastoid muscle（胸鎖乳突肌）** 前緣、**trapezius muscle（斜方肌）** 後緣及**clavicle（鎖骨）** 下緣所構成。區域內包含多條**肌肉**、**血管**與**神經**，為臨床檢查與手術的重要標的。

---

## 肌肉（Muscles）

許多肌肉參與構成頸後三角的邊界與底部（表 8.15）。

### Omohyoid Muscle（下頷舌骨肌）

**Omohyoid（下頷舌骨肌）** 穿越頸後三角的下半部，隨後消失於胸鎖乳突肌下方，重新出現在**anterior triangle（前三角）**。

- **起源（Origin）**：**Scapula（肩胛骨）** 上緣，**scapular notch（肩胛切跡）** 內側
- **終止（Insertion）**：**Hyoid bone（舌骨）** 體部下緣
- **結構**：有兩個肌腹，由肌腱連接，肌腱藉由筋膜懸帶錨定於**clavicle（鎖骨）**
  - **Superior belly（上腹）**：位於前三角
  - **Inferior belly（下腹）**：穿越頸後三角，將其細分為：
    - 下方較小的 **omoclavicular triangle（肩鎖三角）** 或 **subclavian triangle（鎖骨下三角）**
    - 上方較大的 **occipital triangle（枕三角）**
- **神經支配（Innervation）**：**Ansa cervicalis（頸袢）** 分支（C1–C3）
- **功能（Function）**：下降**hyoid bone（舌骨）**

> **臨床重點**：Omohyoid 的解剖路徑具臨床意義，其被**investing layer of cervical fascia（頸部包被筋膜）** 包覆，穿越頸後三角時可作為手術定位的參考標誌。

### 表 8.15：頸後三角相關肌肉

| 肌肉（Muscle） | 起源（Origin） | 終止（Insertion） | 神經支配（Innervation） | 功能（Function） |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Sternocleidomastoid（胸鎖乳突肌）** | 胸骨頭：manubrium（胸骨柄）前面上部；鎖骨頭：鎖骨內側 1/3 | 乳突 process 及 **superior nuchal line（枕骨上項線）** 外側 1/2 | **Accessory nerve [XI]** 及 C2–C3 前支 | 單側：頭側傾向同側，臉轉向對側；雙側：向前拉頭部 |
| **Trapezius（斜方肌）** | 上項線、外側枕骨隆起、**ligamentum nuchae（項韌帶）**、CVII–TXII 棘突 | 鎖骨外側 1/3、**acromion（肩峰）**、**scapula spine（肩胛棘）** | 運動：**Accessory nerve [XI]**；本體感覺：C3–C4 | 協助肩胛骨旋轉；上纖維上提、中纖維內收、下纖維下壓肩胛骨 |
| **Splenius capitis（頭棘肌）** | **Ligamentum nuchae（項韌帶）** 下半部、CVII–TIV 棘突 | 乳突、枕骨上項線外側 1/3 | 頸神經中支後支 | 雙側：向後拉頭部；單側：將頭拉向同側並旋轉 |
| **Levator scapulae（肩胛提肌）** | C1–C4 橫突 | 肩胛骨內側緣上部 | **Dorsal scapular nerve（C4–C5）** | 上提肩胛骨 |
| **Posterior scalene（後斜角肌）** | CIV–CVI 橫突後結節 | 第二肋上表面 | C5–C7 前支 | 上提第二肋 |
| **Middle scalene（中斜角肌）** | CII–CVII 橫突 | 第一肋上表面（鎖骨下动脉溝後方） | C3–C7 前支 | 上提第一肋 |
| **Anterior scalene（前斜角肌）** | CIII–CVI 橫突前結節 | 第一肋 **scalene tubercle（斜角肌結節）** | C4–C7 前支 | 上提第一肋 |
| **Omohyoid（下頷舌骨肌）** | 肩胛骨上緣（肩胛切跡內側） | 舌骨體下緣 | **Ansa cervicalis（C1–C3）** | 下降舌骨 |

---

## 血管（Vessels）

### External Jugular Vein（頸外靜脈）

**External jugular vein（頸外靜脈）** 是穿越頸後三角最淺層的重要結構之一。

- **形成**：於下頷角附近，由 **retromandibular vein（下頷後靜脈）** 後支與 **posterior auricular vein（耳後靜脈）** 匯合而成
- **路徑**：經**sternocleidomastoid（胸鎖乳突肌）** 表面進入頸後三角，垂直下行
- **終點**：在頸後三角下部穿過 **investing layer of cervical fascia（頸部包被筋膜）**，注入**subclavian vein（鎖骨下靜脈）**
- **支流（Tributaries）**：**Transverse cervical vein（橫頸靜脈）**、**suprascapular vein（肩胛上靜脈）**、**anterior jugular vein（頸前靜脈）**

> **臨床重點**：External jugular vein 的淺層位置使其易於觸及，臨床上常用於靜脈穿刺及中心靜脈導管置入的定位參考。

### Subclavian Artery（鎖骨下動脈）及其分支

**Subclavian artery（鎖骨下動脈）** 是頸後三角內最大的动脉，依其與**anterior scalene muscle（前斜角肌）** 的關係分為三部分：

#### 第一部分（First Part）
- 位於**anterior scalene（前斜角肌）** 內側緣
- 右側：起自 **brachiocephalic trunk（頭臂幹）**
- 左側：直接起自**aortic arch（主動脈弓）**
- 眾多分數

#### 第二部分（Second Part）
- 穿行於**anterior scalene（前斜角肌）** 與 **middle scalene（中斜角肌）** 之間
- 可有一條分支由此發出

#### 第三部分（Third Part）
- 從**anterior scalene（前斜角肌）** 與 **middle scalene（中斜角肌）** 間穿出
- 跨越頸後三角底部
- 行程：前斜角肌外側緣 → 第一肋外側緣（延續為 **axillary artery（腋动脉）**）
- **Dorsal scapular artery（肩胛背动脉）**：可能由此部分發出，走向肩胛骨上角，沿內側緣下行

### Transverse Cervical and Suprascapular Arteries（橫頸动脉與肩胛上动脉）

兩者均為 **thyrocervical trunk（甲狀頸幹）** 的分支（甲狀頸幹起自第一部分）：

#### Transverse Cervical Artery（橫頸动脉）
- 行程：從甲狀頸幹發出後，橫向略向後穿過頸後三角底部（位於前斜角肌及**brachial plexus（臂叢）** 前方）
- 終點：到達 **trapezius（斜方肌）** 深面後分為：
  - **淺支（Superficial branch）**：續行於斜方肌深面
  - **深支（Deep branch）**：續行於**rhomboid muscles（菱形肌）** 深面，接近肩胛骨內側緣

#### Suprascapular Artery（肩胛上动脉）
- 行程：略向下斜行，穿越頸後三角最下部，隨後位於**clavicle（鎖骨）** 後方
- 終點：越過**superior transverse scapular ligament（肩胛上橫韌帶）**，分布於肩胛骨後面各肌

> **臨床重點**：這些小动脉的解剖變異常見，術前影像評估對甲狀腺、頸部淋巴結及乳腺手術具重要意義。

### 靜脈（Veins）

所有上述动脉皆有伴行靜脈：

- **Subclavian vein（鎖骨下靜脈）**：為 **axillary vein（腋靜脈）** 的延續，起於第一肋外側緣
  - 經過頸後三角時，**external jugular vein（頸外靜脈）**、**suprascapular vein（肩胛上靜脈）** 及 **transverse cervical vein（橫頸靜脈）** 注入
  - 於**sternoclavicular joint（胸鎖關節）** 附近與**internal jugular vein（頸內靜脈）** 匯合成**brachiocephalic vein（頭臂靜脈）**
  - 位於 **subclavian artery（鎖骨下动脉）** 前方，略低於动脉，並通過前斜角肌前方

---

## 神經（Nerves）

### Accessory Nerve [XI]（副神經）

- **路徑**：穿**jugular foramen（頸靜脈孔）** 離開顱腔後，沿頸部後方下行至**sternocleidomastoid（胸鎖乳突肌）** 前緣
  - 可經該肌深方或穿過並支配之
  - 隨後進入頸後三角，斜向下穿越，行走於**investing layer of cervical fascia（頸部包被筋膜）** 內（該筋膜跨越胸鎖乳突肌與**trapezius（斜方肌）** 之間）
- **終點**：到達**trapezius（斜方肌）** 前緣，繼續行走於斜方肌深面並支配該肌

> **臨床重點**：Accessory nerve [XI] 穿越頸後三角時位置淺表，極易受損。臨床上可導致 **trapezius（斜方肌）** 麻痺，造成肩下垂及手臂外展受限（因無法正常旋轉肩胛骨）。

### Cervical Plexus（頸叢）

**Cervical plexus（頸叢）** 由 **C1–C4（前支）** 形成：

- 位於頸後三角底部肌肉內，**prevertebral layer of cervical fascia（椎前筋膜）** 深面
- 分支包括：
  - **Muscular（深部）分支**
  - **Cutaneous（淺部）分支**

#### 運動（深部）分支

- **Phrenic nerve（膈神經）**：支配**diaphragm（橫膈）** 的感覺與運動
  - 起源：C3–C5 前支
  - 行程：繞過**anterior scalene（前斜角肌）** 上外側緣，於椎前筋膜深面下行穿入胸腔
  - 特徵：在頸部下降時被**transverse cervical（橫頸动脉）** 及 **suprascapular artery（肩胛上动脉）** 固定於前斜角肌

其他供應**prevertebral（前傾）** 及 **lateral vertebral（外側椎骨）** 肌肉的分支，包括：**rectus capitis anterior（前頭直肌）**、**rectus capitis lateralis（外側頭直肌）**、**longus colli（頸長肌）**、**longus capitis（頭長肌）**（表 8.16）。

- **Ansa cervicalis（頸袢）**：由 C1–C3 前支組成，支配**infrahyoid muscles（舌骨下肌）**

#### 表 8.16：椎前與外側椎骨肌肉

| 肌肉（Muscle） | 起源（Origin） | 終止（Insertion） | 神經支配（Innervation） | 功能（Function） |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **Rectus capitis anterior（前頭直肌）** | 寰椎及其橫突前面外側部 | 枕骨底部下面 | C1–C2 前支分支 | 頭部於寰枕關節屈曲 |
| **Rectus capitis lateralis（外側頭直肌）** | 寰椎橫突上面 | 枕骨 jugular process 下面 | C1–C2 前支分支 | 頭部向同側屈曲 |
| **Longus colli（頸長肌）** | 上斜部：CIII–CV 橫突前結節 → 寰椎前弓結節；下斜部：TI–TIII 椎體前面 → CV–CVI 橫突前結節；垂直部：TI–TIII 椎體前面 → CII–CIV 椎體前面 | C2–C6 前支分支 | 頸部向前並向側方屈曲，輕度旋轉至對側 |
| **Longus capitis（頭長肌）** | CIII–CVI 橫突的腱性附著 | 枕骨底部下面 | C1–C3 前支分支 | 屈頭 |

#### 皮質（淺部）分支

皮質分支於胸鎖乳突肌後緣下方穿出，於頸後三角可見：

- **Lesser occipital nerve（小枕神經）**：來自 C2，沿胸鎖乳突肌後緣上行，分布於耳後頸部及頭部後方头皮
- **Great auricular nerve（大耳神經）**：來自 C2–C3，於胸鎖乳突肌後緣發出後上行越過該肌，供應腮腺區、耳及乳突區皮肤
- **Transverse cervical nerve（頸橫神經）**：來自 C2–C3，繞過胸鎖乳突肌中部，水平橫過該肌，供應頸側部及前部
- **Supraclavicular nerves（鎖骨上神經）**：來自 C3–C4，於胸鎖乳突肌後緣下方發出後下行，供應鎖骨及肩上區域（遠至第二肋）

### Brachial Plexus（臂叢）

**Brachial plexus（臂叢）** 由 **C5–C8** 及 **T1** 前支形成：

- **Roots（根）**：位於前斜角肌與中斜角肌之間
- **Trunks（幹）**：
  - **Upper trunk（上幹）**：C5–C6 前支
  - **Middle trunk（中幹）**：C7 前支
  - **Lower trunk（下幹）**：C8–T1 前支
- **行程**：跨越頸後三角底部
- **於頸後三角可見的分支**：
  - **Dorsal scapular nerve（肩胛背神經）**：支配菱形肌
  - **Long thoracic nerve（胸長神經）**：支配 **serratus anterior（前鋸肌）**
  - **Nerve to subclavius（鎖骨下肌神經）**：支配 **subclavius（鎖骨下肌）**
  - **Suprascapular nerve（肩胛上神經）**：支配 **supraspinatus（岡上肌）** 及 **infraspinatus（岡下肌）**

---

## 頸根部（Root of the Neck）

**Root of the neck（頸根部）** 為緊鄰**superior thoracic aperture（胸廓上口）** 及 **axillary inlets（腋窩入口）** 上方的區域。

### 邊界

- **前方（Anterior）**：胸骨柄上緣及鎖骨上緣
- **後方（Posterior）**：第一胸椎上緣及肩胛骨上緣至 **coracoid process（喙突）**

### 內容物

此區包含於頸、胸、上肢間流通的結構。此外，有胸腔頂部向上延伸至頸根部：

- **Cupula（胸膜頂）**：壁層胸膜的頸部部分，覆蓋肺尖
- **Apical part of superior lobe（肺上葉尖部）**：每側肺尖

> **臨床重點**：胸膜頂前方高於胸骨柄上緣及第一肋上緣（因鎖骨及第一肋遮擋）；後方因胸廓上口向下斜行，胸膜頂低於第一胸椎上緣。臨床上，頸根部為頸廓清術及中心靜脈穿刺的重要區域。

### Subclavian Arteries（鎖骨下动脉）

兩側 **subclavian artery（鎖骨下动脉）** 均向上拱起離開胸腔進入頸根部：

- **Right subclavian artery（右鎖骨下动脉）**：
  - 起點：位於胸鎖關節後方，為**brachiocephalic trunk（頭臂幹）** 的兩終支之一
  - 行程：向上向外拱起，經頸根部胸膜頂前方、**anterior scalene（前斜角肌）** 後方
  - 延續：越過第一肋外側後延續為 **axillary artery（腋动脉）**

- **Left subclavian artery（左鎖骨下动脉）**：
  - 起點：低於右側，直接起自**aortic arch（主動脈弓）**
  - 行程：位於左**common carotid artery（頸總动脉）** 後方、氣管外側，向上拱起後穿行於胸膜頂前方及前斜角肌後方
  - 延續：越過第一肋外側後延續為腋动脉

---

## 總結

頸後三角容納多個重要解剖結構：

- **肌肉**：Sternocleidomastoid、trapezius、splenius capitis、scalenes、omohyoid 等
- **靜脈**：External jugular vein 為最淺層標誌
- **动脉**：Subclavian artery 第三部分及其分支（transverse cervical、suprascapular、dorsal scapular）
- **神經**：Accessory nerve [XI]、cervical plexus（皮質支及運動支）、brachial plexus
- **頸根部**：連接頸、胸、上肢的樞紐區域

> **臨床重點**：頸後三角內結構因位置淺表而易受創傷，術中識別及保護神經血管為頸部手術成敗關鍵。

# Head and Neck — Segment 38: Root of the Neck & Pharynx

---

## Subclavian Artery

The **subclavian artery** continues laterally over rib I and becomes the **axillary artery** as it crosses the lateral border of rib I.

Both subclavian arteries are divided into three parts by the **anterior scalene muscle** (Fig. 8.187):

- **First part**: extends from the origin of the artery to the anterior scalene muscle.
- **Second part**: the part of the artery posterior to the anterior scalene muscle.
- **Third part**: the part lateral to the anterior scalene muscle before the artery reaches the lateral border of rib I.

All branches from the right and left subclavian arteries arise from the first part of the artery, except for one branch (the **costocervical trunk**) on the right side (Fig. 8.187). The branches include the **vertebral artery**, the **thyrocervical trunk**, the **internal thoracic artery**, and the **costocervical trunk**.

---

### Vertebral Artery

The **vertebral artery** is the first branch of the subclavian artery as it enters the root of the neck (Fig. 8.187). A large branch, arising from the first part of the subclavian artery medial to the **anterior scalene muscle**, it ascends and enters the foramen in the transverse process of vertebra **CVI**. Continuing to pass superiorly, the vertebral artery passes through the foramina of vertebrae **CV to CI**. At the superior border of vertebra **CI**, the artery turns medially and crosses the posterior arch of vertebra **CI**. From here it passes through the **foramen magnum** to enter the posterior cranial fossa.

---

### Thyrocervical Trunk

The second branch of the subclavian artery is the **thyrocervical trunk** (Fig. 8.187). It arises from the first part of the subclavian artery medial to the **anterior scalene muscle**, and divides into three branches: the **inferior thyroid artery**, the **transverse cervical artery**, and the **suprascapular artery**.

#### Inferior Thyroid Artery

The **inferior thyroid artery** (Fig. 8.187) is the superior continuation of the thyrocervical trunk. It ascends, anterior to the **anterior scalene muscle**, and eventually turns medially, crossing posterior to the **carotid sheath** and its contents and anterior to the **vertebral artery**. Reaching the posterior surface of the thyroid gland, it supplies the thyroid gland.

When the inferior thyroid artery turns medially, it gives off an important branch (the **ascending cervical artery**), which continues to ascend on the anterior surface of the prevertebral muscles, supplying these muscles and sending branches to the spinal cord.

#### Transverse Cervical Artery

The middle branch of the thyrocervical trunk is the **transverse cervical artery** (Fig. 8.187). This branch passes laterally, across the anterior surface of the **anterior scalene muscle** and the **phrenic nerve**, and enters and crosses the base of the **posterior triangle of the neck**. It continues to the deep surface of the **trapezius muscle**, where it divides into superficial and deep branches:

- The **superficial branch** continues on the deep surface of the trapezius muscle.
- The **deep branch** continues on the deep surface of the rhomboid muscles near the medial border of the scapula.

#### Suprascapular Artery

The lowest branch of the thyrocervical trunk is the **suprascapular artery** (Fig. 8.187). This branch passes laterally, crossing anterior to the **anterior scalene muscle**, the **phrenic nerve**, the third part of the **subclavian artery**, and the trunks of the **brachial plexus**. At the superior border of the scapula, it crosses over the **superior transverse scapular ligament** and enters the **supraspinatus fossa**.

---

### Internal Thoracic Artery

The third branch of the subclavian artery is the **internal thoracic artery** (Fig. 8.187). This artery branches from the inferior edge of the subclavian artery and descends. It passes posterior to the **clavicle** and the large veins in the region and anterior to the pleural cavity. It enters the thoracic cavity posterior to the ribs and anterior to the **transversus thoracis muscle** and continues to descend, giving off numerous branches.

---

### Costocervical Trunk

The final branch of the subclavian artery in the root of the neck is the **costocervical trunk** (Fig. 8.187). It arises in a slightly different position, depending on the side:

- **On the left**: arises from the first part of the subclavian artery, just medial to the **anterior scalene muscle**.
- **On the right**: arises from the second part of the subclavian artery.

On both sides, the costocervical trunk ascends and passes posteriorly over the dome of the pleural cavity and continues in a posterior direction behind the **anterior scalene muscle**. Eventually it divides into two branches: the **deep cervical** and the **supreme intercostal arteries**:

- The **deep cervical artery** ascends in the back of the neck and anastomoses with the descending branch of the **occipital artery**.
- The **supreme intercostal artery** descends anterior to rib I and divides to form the posterior intercostal arteries for the first two intercostal spaces.

---

## Veins

Numerous veins pass through the root of the neck. Small veins accompany each of the arteries described above, and large veins form major drainage channels.

The **subclavian veins** begin at the lateral margin of rib I as continuations of the **axillary veins**. Passing medially on each side, just anterior to the **anterior scalene muscles**, each subclavian vein is joined by the **internal jugular vein** to form the **brachiocephalic veins**.

The only tributary to each subclavian vein is an **external jugular vein**.

The veins accompanying the numerous arteries in this region empty into other veins.

---

## Nerves in the Root of the Neck

### Phrenic Nerves

The **phrenic nerves** are branches of the **cervical plexus** and arise on each side as contributions from the anterior rami of **cervical nerves C3 to C5** come together. Passing around the upper lateral border of each **anterior scalene muscle**, the phrenic nerves continue inferiorly across the anterior surface of each **anterior scalene muscle** within the **prevertebral layer of cervical fascia** (Fig. 8.188). Leaving the lower edge of the **anterior scalene muscle**, each phrenic nerve passes between the **subclavian vein** and **artery** to enter the thorax and continue to the **diaphragm**.

---

### Vagus Nerves [X]

The **vagus nerves [X]** descend through the neck within the **carotid sheath**, posterior to and just between the **common carotid artery** and the **internal jugular vein**.

In the lower part of the neck, the vagus nerves [X] give off **cardiac branches**, which continue downward and medially, passing posterior to the **subclavian arteries** to disappear into the thorax.

In the root of the neck, each vagus nerve [X] passes anterior to the **subclavian artery** and posterior to the **subclavian vein** as it enters the thorax (Fig. 8.188).

---

### Recurrent Laryngeal Nerves

The right and left **recurrent laryngeal nerves** are visible as they originate in (the right recurrent laryngeal nerve) or pass through (the left recurrent laryngeal nerve) the root of the neck.

The **right recurrent laryngeal nerve** is a branch of the right **vagus nerve [X]** as it reaches the lower edge of the first part of the **subclavian artery** in the root of the neck. It passes around the **subclavian artery** and upward and medially in a groove between the **trachea** and the **esophagus** as it heads to the larynx.

The **left recurrent laryngeal nerve** is a branch of the left **vagus nerve [X]** as it crosses the arch of the aorta in the superior mediastinum. It passes below and behind the **arch of the aorta** and ascends beside the **trachea** to the larynx (Fig. 8.188).

> **臨床重點**：**復發性喉返神經麻痺（Recurrent laryngeal nerve palsy）**
>
> 損傷單側或雙側的復發性喉返神經可能導致最初聲音沙啞，最終無法說話。復發性喉返神經麻痺可因沿其路徑任何地方的神經中斷而發生。此外，在復發性喉返神經分支之前中斷迷走神經也可能產生發聲症狀。
>
> 右肺尖的肺癌可影響右側復發性喉返神經，而浸潤至肺動脈和主動脈之間區域（臨床上稱為「主肺窗」）的癌症可影響左側復發性喉返神經甲狀腺手術也可能創傷復發性喉返神經。

---

## Sympathetic Nervous System in the Root of the Neck

Various components of the **sympathetic nervous system** are visible as they pass through the root of the neck (Fig. 8.189). These include:

- The **cervical part of the sympathetic trunk**
- The **ganglia** associated with the cervical part of the sympathetic trunk
- **Cardiac nerves** branching from the cervical part of the sympathetic trunk

The **sympathetic trunks** are two parallel cords that run from the base of the skull to the coccyx. Along the way they are punctuated by **ganglia**, which are collections of neuronal cell bodies outside the CNS.

---

### Cervical Part of the Sympathetic Trunk

The cervical part of the **sympathetic trunk** is anterior to the **longus colli** and **longus capitis muscles**, and posterior to the **common carotid artery** in the carotid sheath and the **internal carotid artery**. It is connected to each cervical spinal nerve by a **gray ramus communicans** (Fig. 8.190). There are no white rami communicantes in the cervical region.

---

### Ganglia of the Sympathetic Trunk

Three ganglia are usually described along the course of the sympathetic trunk in the cervical region, and in these ganglia ascending preganglionic sympathetic fibers from upper thoracic spinal cord levels synapse with postganglionic sympathetic fibers. The postganglionic sympathetic fibers are distributed in branches from these ganglia.

#### Superior Cervical Ganglion

A very large **superior cervical ganglion** in the area of cervical vertebrae **CI and CII** marks the superior extent of the sympathetic trunk (Figs. 8.189 and 8.190). Its branches pass to:

- The **internal carotid** and **external carotid arteries**, forming plexuses around these vessels
- **Cervical spinal nerves C1 to C4** through gray rami communicantes
- The **pharynx**
- The **heart** as **superior cardiac nerves**

#### Middle Cervical Ganglion

A second ganglion inferior to the superior cervical ganglion along the course of the sympathetic trunk (the **middle cervical ganglion**) is encountered at about the level of cervical vertebra **CVI** (Figs. 8.189 and 8.190). Branches from this ganglion pass to:

- **Cervical spinal nerves C5 and C6** through gray rami communicantes
- The **heart** as **middle cardiac nerves**

#### Inferior Cervical Ganglion

At the lower end of the cervical part of the sympathetic trunk is another ganglion (the **inferior cervical ganglion**), which becomes very large when it combines with the first thoracic ganglion and forms the **cervicothoracic ganglion (stellate ganglion)**. The inferior cervical ganglion (Figs. 8.189 and 8.190) is anterior to the neck of rib I and the transverse process of cervical vertebra **CVII**, and posterior to the first part of the **subclavian artery** and the origin of the **vertebral artery**.

Branches from this ganglion pass to:

- **Spinal nerves C7 to T1** through gray rami communicantes
- The **vertebral artery**, forming a plexus associated with this vessel
- The **heart** as **inferior cardiac nerves**

This ganglion may also receive white rami communicantes from thoracic spinal nerve **T1**, and occasionally, from **T2**.

---

## Lymphatics

### Thoracic Duct

The **thoracic duct** is a major lymphatic channel that begins in the abdomen, passes superiorly through the thorax, and ends in the venous channels in the neck. It passes through the lower thoracic cavity in the midline with:

- The **thoracic aorta** on the left
- The **azygos vein** on the right
- The **esophagus** anteriorly

At about the level of thoracic vertebra **TV**, the thoracic duct passes to the left and continues to ascend just to the left of the **esophagus**. It passes through the superior mediastinum and enters the root of the neck to the left of the esophagus (Fig. 8.191). Arching laterally, it passes posterior to the **carotid sheath** and turns inferiorly in front of the **thyrocervical trunk**, the **phrenic nerve**, and the **vertebral artery**.

The **thoracic duct** terminates in the junction between the **left internal jugular** and the **left subclavian veins** (Fig. 8.191). Near its junction with the venous system it is joined by:

- The **left jugular trunk**, which drains lymph from the left side of the head and neck
- The **left subclavian trunk**, which drains lymph from the left upper limb
- Occasionally, the **left bronchomediastinal trunk**, which drains lymph from the left half of the thoracic structures (Fig. 8.192)

A similar confluence of three lymphatic trunks occurs on the right side of the body. Emptying into the junction between the **right internal jugular** and **right subclavian veins** are:

- The **right jugular trunk** from the head and neck
- The **right subclavian trunk** from the right upper limb
- Occasionally, the **right bronchomediastinal trunk** carrying lymph from the structures in the right half of the thoracic cavity and the right upper intercostal spaces (Fig. 8.192)

There is variability in how these trunks enter the veins. They may combine into a single **right lymphatic duct** to enter the venous system or enter as three separate trunks.

---

### Lymphatics of the Neck

A description of the organization of the lymphatic system in the neck becomes a summary of the lymphatic system in the head and neck. It is impossible to separate the two regions. The components of this system include **superficial nodes** around the head, **superficial cervical nodes** along the external jugular vein, and **deep cervical nodes** forming a chain along the internal jugular vein (Fig. 8.193).

The basic pattern of drainage is for superficial lymphatic vessels to drain to the superficial nodes. Some of these drain to the superficial cervical nodes on their way to the deep cervical nodes, and others drain directly to the deep cervical nodes.

---

#### Superficial Lymph Nodes

Five groups of superficial lymph nodes form a ring around the head and are primarily responsible for the lymphatic drainage of the face and scalp. Their pattern of drainage is very similar to the area of distribution of the arteries near their location.

Beginning posteriorly, these groups (Fig. 8.193) are:

- **Occipital nodes**: near the attachment of the **trapezius muscle** to the skull and associated with the **occipital artery** — lymphatic drainage is from the posterior scalp and neck
- **Mastoid nodes (retro-auricular/posterior auricular nodes)**: posterior to the ear near the attachment of the **sternocleidomastoid muscle** and associated with the **posterior auricular artery** — lymphatic drainage is from the posterolateral half of the scalp
- **Pre-auricular and parotid nodes**: anterior to the ear and associated with the **superficial temporal** and **transverse facial arteries** — lymphatic drainage is from the anterior surface of the auricle, the anterolateral scalp, the upper half of the face, the eyelids, and the cheeks
- **Submandibular nodes**: inferior to the body of the **mandible** and associated with the **facial artery** — lymphatic drainage is from structures along the path of the facial artery as high as the forehead, as well as the gingivae, the teeth, and the tongue
- **Submental nodes**: inferior and posterior to the chin — lymphatic drainage is from the center part of the lower lip, the chin, the floor of the mouth, the tip of the tongue, and the lower incisor teeth

Lymphatic flow from these superficial lymph nodes passes in several directions:

- Drainage from the **occipital** and **mastoid nodes** passes to the superficial cervical nodes along the **external jugular vein**
- Drainage from the **pre-auricular and parotid nodes**, the **submandibular nodes**, and the **submental nodes** passes to the **deep cervical nodes**

---

#### Superficial Cervical Lymph Nodes

The **superficial cervical nodes** are a collection of lymph nodes along the **external jugular vein** on the superficial surface of the **sternocleidomastoid muscle** (Fig. 8.193). They primarily receive lymphatic drainage from the posterior and posterolateral regions of the scalp through the **occipital** and **mastoid nodes**, and send lymphatic vessels in the direction of the **deep cervical nodes**.

---

#### Deep Cervical Lymph Nodes

The **deep cervical nodes** are a collection of lymph nodes that form a chain along the **internal jugular vein** (Fig. 8.193). They are divided into upper and lower groups where the intermediate tendon of the **omohyoid muscle** crosses the **common carotid artery** and the **internal jugular vein**.

The most superior node in the upper deep cervical group is the **jugulodigastric node** (Fig. 8.193). This large node is where the **posterior belly of the digastric muscle** crosses the **internal jugular vein** and receives lymphatic drainage from the **tonsils** and tonsillar region.

Another large node, usually associated with the lower deep cervical group because it is at or just inferior to the intermediate tendon of the **omohyoid muscle**, is the **jugulo-omohyoid node** (Fig. 8.193). This node receives lymphatic drainage from the **tongue**.

The deep cervical nodes eventually receive all lymphatic drainage from the head and neck either directly or through regional groups of nodes.
